The\n85\tpattern accepts canonical eval ids (``E1``, ``E15``, ``D15``) and\n86\tparameterize-expanded ids (``E2.1``, ``E2.10``) while rejecting:\n87\t\n88\t* Path-traversal patterns: ``../home``, ``/etc``, ``..``, ``./foo``.\n89\t* Embedded separators: ``foo/bar``, ``foo\\\\bar``, ``E1/x``.\n90\t* Empty strings and leading-digit ids (``1E``, ``9``).\n91\t* Template tokens that leaked past parameterize expansion\n92\t  (``{{prefix}}``, ``${var}``).\n93\t\n94\tThe pattern is compiled at module import so the loader hot path (one\n95\t:func:`validate_eval_id` call per eval, plus one per parameterize-\n96\texpanded id) avoids re-parsing on every check.\n97\t\"\"\"\n98\t\n99\t\n100\tclass InvalidEvalId(Exception):\n101\t    \"\"\"Raised when an eval id fails the FR-SCH2 regex.\n102\t\n103\t    The guard is applied in two places by COMP-002 ``SuiteLoader`` (T01.07):\n104\t\n105\t    1. At loader entry, against every static id in the manifest. (The\n106\t       schema layer rejects malformed static ids too, but the runtime\n107\t       guard is the authoritative check the security model relies on.)\n108\t    2. After parameterize expansion, against every generated id (``E2.1``,\n109\t       ``E2.2``, ...). Phase 1 is the **viability gate**: if vendored `ptytest` can drive a real Claude Code TTY subprocess and `HomeIsolation` provides clean per-eval `HOME` isolation, the rest of the harness (Phases 2-5) is straightforward. If Phase 1 fails, the architecture pivots. The task file ends with a working `uv run superclaude eval doctor` subcommand that prints a green-checklist verification of all hard requirements (claude binary, make, jq, git) and optional capabilities (3 MCP servers).\n60\t\n61\tThe task uses **Template 02 (Complex Task)** because it requires discovery before building (decisions log + design-spec must be cross-checked against current source line ranges), parallel-style independent build streams (vendored `pty/`, `isolation.py`, `capability_gates.py`, and `commands.py` are largely independent files), test/execute steps (vendored pytest suite must pass), QA gate checkpoints between major implementation phases (PG-1 through PG-4 per the I15/I16 convention), and conditional fix cycles (failed QA gates trigger up to 3 fix cycles per gate type with the Retry Monotonicity Protocol from rf-task-builder Critical Rule 14 applied). Output target line count is **~400 LOC** across 14 new files (per design-spec §17 Phase 1 estimate: pty/ ~150 LOC adapted, isolation.py ~120 LOC, capability_gates.py ~80 LOC, commands.py doctor-only ~50 LOC, tests ~150-200 LOC across 3 files).\n62\t\n63\tThe task file is **NOT runnable by Sprint CLI** — it is built for manual execution via the `/task` skill per the user's 2026-05-18 directive. The branch for execution will be `feat/cliEval-P1-pty-isolation-gates` and the PR title will be `feat(eval): cliEval P1 — vendor ptytest + HomeIsolation + capability gates + eval doctor`.\n64\t\n65\t## Key Objectives\n66\t\n67\tThe following objectives MUST be achieved by this task. Each maps to one or more of the 8 acceptance criteria from the BUILD_REQUEST (AC-P1.1 through AC-P1.8) — see the **Acceptance Criteria Mapping** section below for the per-AC checklist-item index.\n68\t\n69\t1. **Vendor brandon-fryslie/ptytest fork under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`** with the upstream MIT LICENSE preserved verbatim, PROVENANCE.md documenting fork SHA + rename PtySession→PtyDriver + removed pytest-fixture autoloader + added expect_prompt_ready/inject_prompt + tightened pexpect>=4.9 + aggressive ANSI strip (AC-P1.4, AC-P1.5).\n70\t2. **Implement `HomeIsolation` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/isolation.py`** as a class that COMPOSES (has-a, not is-a) the existing `IsolationLayers` from `src/superclaude/cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182`, adding HOME / XDG_* / CLAUDE_SESSION_ID overrides; `setup()` deploys 9 hook scripts via `install_hooks` + writes settings.json + seeds requested state files; `env()` returns the env-var overlay including passthrough of PATH/USER/TERM/LANG/CI; `teardown(keep=False)` rm-rf's the dir unless keep=True (AC-P1.2, AC-P1.6).\n71\t3. **Implement `capability_gates.py`** with the `Capability` dataclass + `CAPABILITIES` registry (4 hard binaries + 3 optional MCP servers per design-spec §11) + `check_all(skip_flags)` returning a `CapabilityReport` consumed by `eval doctor` (AC-P1.1).\n72\t4. **Implement Click sub-package `cli/eval/`** with `__init__.py` exporting `eval_group`, `config.py` with `EvalConfig` dataclass, and `commands.py` with ONLY the `eval doctor` subcommand wired (`eval run`, `eval list`, `eval describe` are explicitly out-of-scope for Phase 1 — they land in P2/P3) (AC-P1.1).\n73\t5. **Add three pytest test files under `tests/cli/test_eval/`**: `test_isolation.py` (HOME override correctness + setup/teardown idempotence), `test_capability_gates.py` (all gates including missing-binary failure modes), `test_pty_vendor.py` (vendored ptytest's own upstream test suite still passes after our diffs) (AC-P1.3, AC-P1.4, AC-P1.8).\n74\t6. **Hard guard in `HomeIsolation.setup()`** that REFUSES to operate if `home_root` is outside a known eval-runs scratch dir (e.g., `/tmp/eval-runs/...` or `.dev/eval-runs/...`) — prevents the foot-gun where a typo blows away the real `~/.claude/` (AC-P1.6).\n75\t7. **No regression to `make verify-sync`** — after this phase lands, `make verify-sync` MUST still exit 0. The PRE/POST verify-sync runs are encoded as explicit validation items (AC-P1.7).\n76\t8. **All new tests pass** — `uv run pytest tests/cli/test_eval/test_isolation.py tests/cli/test_eval/test_capability_gates.py tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py -v` returns 0 with all tests PASS (AC-P1.8).\n77\t\n78\t## Prerequisites & Dependencies\n79\t\n80\t### Parent Task & Dependencies\n81\t- **Parent Task:** None (first phase of cliEval release; no upstream task).\n82\t- **Blocking Dependencies:** None at task-start — the design-spec and decisions log are already published; the upstream ptytest repo at https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest is publicly reachable.\n83\t- **This task blocks:** cliEval Phase 2 (loader.py + models.py + expect.py + eval describe/list), Phase 3 (orchestrator.py + runner.py + reporter.py + eval run), Phase 4 (wire into cli/main.py + Makefile + .gitignore), and Phase 5 (eval body implementations E1-E15).\n84\t\n85\t### Required Inputs (read in Phase 1)\n86\t\n87\tThe Stage A-equivalent (design + decisions + BUILD_REQUEST) has already produced the inputs listed below. The primary heuristic SHOULD be a compiled regex that matches one of `^> $`, `^\\$ $`, `^claude> $`, or `^❯ $` (whichever is observed in any captured Claude Code TTY transcript referenced in the repo — search `.dev/` for `*.log` files containing claude-code output). Phase 1 is the **viability gate**: if vendored `ptytest` can drive a real Claude Code TTY subprocess and `HomeIsolation` provides clean per-eval `HOME` isolation, the rest of the harness (Phases 2-5) is straightforward. If Phase 1 fails, the architecture pivots. The task file ends with a working `uv run superclaude eval doctor` subcommand that prints a green-checklist verification of all hard requirements (claude binary, make, jq, git) and optional capabilities (3 MCP servers).\n\nThe task uses **Template 02 (Complex Task)** because it requires discovery before building (decisions log + design-spec must be cross-checked against current source line ranges), parallel-style independent build streams (vendored `pty/`, `isolation.py`, `capability_gates.py`, and `commands.py` are largely independent files), test/execute steps (vendored pytest suite must pass), QA gate checkpoints between major implementation phases (PG-1 through PG-4 per the I15/I16 convention), and conditional fix cycles (failed QA gates trigger up to 3 fix cycles per gate type with the Retry Monotonicity Protocol from rf-task-builder Critical Rule 14 applied). Output target line count is **~400 LOC** across 14 new files (per design-spec §17 Phase 1 estimate: pty/ ~150 LOC adapted, isolation.py ~120 LOC, capability_gates.py ~80 LOC, commands.py doctor-only ~50 LOC, tests ~150-200 LOC across 3 files).\n\nThe task file is **NOT runnable by Sprint CLI** — it is built for manual execution via the `/task` skill per the user's 2026-05-18 directive. The branch for execution will be `feat/cliEval-P1-pty-isolation-gates` and the PR title will be `feat(eval): cliEval P1 — vendor ptytest + HomeIsolation + capability gates + eval doctor`.\n\n## Key Objectives\n\nThe following objectives MUST be achieved by this task. Each maps to one or more of the 8 acceptance criteria from the BUILD_REQUEST (AC-P1.1 through AC-P1.8) — see the **Acceptance Criteria Mapping** section below for the per-AC checklist-item index.\n\n1. **Vendor brandon-fryslie/ptytest fork under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`** with the upstream MIT LICENSE preserved verbatim, PROVENANCE.md documenting fork SHA + rename PtySession→PtyDriver + removed pytest-fixture autoloader + added expect_prompt_ready/inject_prompt + tightened pexpect>=4.9 + aggressive ANSI strip (AC-P1.4, AC-P1.5).\n2. **Implement `HomeIsolation` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/isolation.py`** as a class that COMPOSES (has-a, not is-a) the existing `IsolationLayers` from `src/superclaude/cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182`, adding HOME / XDG_* / CLAUDE_SESSION_ID overrides; `setup()` deploys 9 hook scripts via `install_hooks` + writes settings.json + seeds requested state files; `env()` returns the env-var overlay including passthrough of PATH/USER/TERM/LANG/CI; `teardown(keep=False)` rm-rf's the dir unless keep=True (AC-P1.2, AC-P1.6).\n3. **Implement `capability_gates.py`** with the `Capability` dataclass + `CAPABILITIES` registry (4 hard binaries + 3 optional MCP servers per design-spec §11) + `check_all(skip_flags)` returning a `CapabilityReport` consumed by `eval doctor` (AC-P1.1).\n4. **Implement Click sub-package `cli/eval/`** with `__init__.py` exporting `eval_group`, `config.py` with `EvalConfig` dataclass, and `commands.py` with ONLY the `eval doctor` subcommand wired (`eval run`, `eval list`, `eval describe` are explicitly out-of-scope for Phase 1 — they land in P2/P3) (AC-P1.1).\n5. **Add three pytest test files under `tests/cli/test_eval/`**: `test_isolation.py` (HOME override correctness + setup/teardown idempotence), `test_capability_gates.py` (all gates including missing-binary failure modes), `test_pty_vendor.py` (vendored ptytest's own upstream test suite still passes after our diffs) (AC-P1.3, AC-P1.4, AC-P1.8).\n6. **Hard guard in `HomeIsolation.setup()`** that REFUSES to operate if `home_root` is outside a known eval-runs scratch dir (e.g., `/tmp/eval-runs/...` or `.dev/eval-runs/...`) — prevents the foot-gun where a typo blows away the real `~/.claude/` (AC-P1.6).\n7. **No regression to `make verify-sync`** — after this phase lands, `make verify-sync` MUST still exit 0. The PRE/POST verify-sync runs are encoded as explicit validation items (AC-P1.7).\n8. **All new tests pass** — `uv run pytest tests/cli/test_eval/test_isolation.py tests/cli/test_eval/test_capability_gates.py tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py -v` returns 0 with all tests PASS (AC-P1.8).\n\n## Prerequisites & Dependencies\n\n### Parent Task & Dependencies\n- **Parent Task:** None (first phase of cliEval release; no upstream task).\n- **Blocking Dependencies:** None at task-start — the design-spec and decisions log are already published; the upstream ptytest repo at https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest is publicly reachable.\n- **This task blocks:** cliEval Phase 2 (loader.py + models.py + expect.py + eval describe/list), Phase 3 (orchestrator.py + runner.py + reporter.py + eval run), Phase 4 (wire into cli/main.py + Makefile + .gitignore), and Phase 5 (eval body implementations E1-E15).\n\n### Required Inputs (read in Phase 1)\n\nThe Stage A-equivalent (design + decisions + BUILD_REQUEST) has already produced the inputs listed below. The function accepts\n17\t  any value at runtime so non-string scalars (`int`, `bool`, `None`,\n18\t  `bytes`) that may leak through YAML decoding are rejected via the\n19\t  type guard rather than slipping past the regex check.\n20\t- **Output:** `None` on success (the function is a pure guard).\n21\t- **Error behaviour:** raises `InvalidEvalId` carrying the offending\n22\t  value on `InvalidEvalId.eval_id`. CLI callers MUST map this to\n23\t  `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n24\t\n25\t## The regex\n26\t\n27\t```python\n28\tEVAL_ID_REGEX = re.compile(r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\")\n29\t```\n30\t\n31\tThis is the single source of truth for the runtime guard. The same\n32\tregex literal is encoded into `suites/suite.schema.json` (T01.02) as\n33\tthe `evalIdString` `pattern`, so schema validation rejects unsafe\n34\t**static** ids at the schema layer too; `validate_eval_id` is the\n35\tauthoritative runtime check the security model relies on (especially\n36\tfor post-parameterize-expanded ids that never touch the schema).\n37\t\n38\tThe pattern accepts:\n39\t\n40\t| Shape           | Examples                          |\n41\t|-----------------|-----------------------------------|\n42\t| Canonical eval  | `E1`, `E2`, `E15`, `D15`          |\n43\t| Parameter-ized  | `E2.1`, `E2.2`, `E2.10`, `Foo42.7`|\n44\t| Single letter   | `A`, `Z9`                         |\n45\t| Mixed-case body | `Test1`, `ABC123`                 |\n46\t\n47\tIt rejects everything else; the named negative cases below are the\n48\tfloor, not the ceiling.\n49\t\n50\t## Negative-case checklist (FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1)\n51\t\n52\t| Category                  | Examples                                                 | Why it must fail                                              |\n53\t|---------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|\n54\t| Empty string              | `\"\"`                                                     | AC bullet; trivially escapes `home_root / \"\" / \"home\"`        |\n55\t| Path-traversal prefix     | `../home`, `../../etc`, `..`                             | AC bullet; escapes the scratch root via `Path` join semantics |\n56\t| Absolute path             | `/etc`, `/tmp/eval-runs`                                 | AC bullet; `Path(\"/abs\") / \"home\"` discards `home_root`       |\n57\t| Embedded separator        | `foo/bar`, `foo\\\\bar`, `E1/x`, `./foo`, `.`              | Lets attacker carve sub-directories under `home_root`         |\n58\t| Leading-digit id          | `1E`, `9`, `0E`, `1`, `12.3`                             | AC bullet; the schema also bans this — runtime mirrors        |\n59\t| Template token leakage    | `{{prefix}}`, `E{{p}}`, `${var}`, `<id>`, `%name%`       | AC bullet; an un-substituted token must NEVER reach the FS    |\n60\t| Whitespace / control char | ` E1`, `E1 `, `E 1`, `\\tE1`, `E1\\n`, `E1\\x00`            | Defence in depth — control chars confuse downstream consumers |\n61\t| Wrong character class     | `e1`, `eval1`, `E-1`, `E_1`                              | Underscores and lowercase are excluded by the regex anchor    |\n62\t| Malformed decimal         | `E1.`, `E.1`, `E1..1`, `E1.1.1`, `E1.1.`                 | Only one `.N` decimal allowed (mirrors parameterize semantics)|\n63\t| Non-string scalar         | `None`, `1`, `1.0`, `b\"E1\"`, `[\"E1\"]`, `(\"E1\",)`, `{...}`| YAML may decode bare scalars; type guard runs before regex    |\n64\t\n65\t> All entries above are exercised by `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py`\n66\t> (T01.05 unit surface, 66 assertions). The dedicated path-traversal\n67\t> negative-case test set (T01.08 / NFR-SEC1) cross-links the same\n68\t> categories at the integration layer.\n69\t\n70\t## Error → exit-code mapping\n71\t\n72\t| Trigger                                      | Raised           | Exit code                  |\n73\t|----------------------------------------------|------------------|----------------------------|\n74\t| `eval_id` is not `str`                       | `InvalidEvalId`  | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`|\n75\t| `eval_id` does not match `EVAL_ID_REGEX`     | `InvalidEvalId`  | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`|\n76\t\n77\t`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE = 2` is kept as its own constant (even though\n78\tthe value equals `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`) so call sites can branch on\n79\tthe *intent* of the rejection without coupling the two failure classes\n80\ttogether. Both surface to operators as the same \"harness rejected the\n81\tmanifest before any filesystem write\" outcome (design-spec §4).\n82\t\n83\t## Application sites (FR-SCH2 ordering)\n84\t\n85\t`validate_eval_id` is invoked in two places by the SuiteLoader\n86\torchestrator (T01.07 / COMP-002):\n87\t\n88\t1. **At loader entry**, against every static `evals[].id` in the parsed\n89\t   manifest. The function accepts\n  any value at runtime so non-string scalars (`int`, `bool`, `None`,\n  `bytes`) that may leak through YAML decoding are rejected via the\n  type guard rather than slipping past the regex check.\n- **Output:** `None` on success (the function is a pure guard).\n- **Error behaviour:** raises `InvalidEvalId` carrying the offending\n  value on `InvalidEvalId.eval_id`. CLI callers MUST map this to\n  `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n## The regex\n\n```python\nEVAL_ID_REGEX = re.compile(r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\")\n```\n\nThis is the single source of truth for the runtime guard. The same\nregex literal is encoded into `suites/suite.schema.json` (T01.02) as\nthe `evalIdString` `pattern`, so schema validation rejects unsafe\n**static** ids at the schema layer too; `validate_eval_id` is the\nauthoritative runtime check the security model relies on (especially\nfor post-parameterize-expanded ids that never touch the schema).\n\nThe pattern accepts:\n\n| Shape           | Examples                          |\n|-----------------|-----------------------------------|\n| Canonical eval  | `E1`, `E2`, `E15`, `D15`          |\n| Parameter-ized  | `E2.1`, `E2.2`, `E2.10`, `Foo42.7`|\n| Single letter   | `A`, `Z9`                         |\n| Mixed-case body | `Test1`, `ABC123`                 |\n\nIt rejects everything else; the named negative cases below are the\nfloor, not the ceiling.\n\n## Negative-case checklist (FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1)\n\n| Category                  | Examples                                                 | Why it must fail                                              |\n|---------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|\n| Empty string              | `\"\"`                                                     | AC bullet; trivially escapes `home_root / \"\" / \"home\"`        |\n| Path-traversal prefix     | `../home`, `../../etc`, `..`                             | AC bullet; escapes the scratch root via `Path` join semantics |\n| Absolute path             | `/etc`, `/tmp/eval-runs`                                 | AC bullet; `Path(\"/abs\") / \"home\"` discards `home_root`       |\n| Embedded separator        | `foo/bar`, `foo\\\\bar`, `E1/x`, `./foo`, `.`              | Lets attacker carve sub-directories under `home_root`         |\n| Leading-digit id          | `1E`, `9`, `0E`, `1`, `12.3`                             | AC bullet; the schema also bans this — runtime mirrors        |\n| Template token leakage    | `{{prefix}}`, `E{{p}}`, `${var}`, `<id>`, `%name%`       | AC bullet; an un-substituted token must NEVER reach the FS    |\n| Whitespace / control char | ` E1`, `E1 `, `E 1`, `\\tE1`, `E1\\n`, `E1\\x00`            | Defence in depth — control chars confuse downstream consumers |\n| Wrong character class     | `e1`, `eval1`, `E-1`, `E_1`                              | Underscores and lowercase are excluded by the regex anchor    |\n| Malformed decimal         | `E1.`, `E.1`, `E1..1`, `E1.1.1`, `E1.1.`                 | Only one `.N` decimal allowed (mirrors parameterize semantics)|\n| Non-string scalar         | `None`, `1`, `1.0`, `b\"E1\"`, `[\"E1\"]`, `(\"E1\",)`, `{...}`| YAML may decode bare scalars; type guard runs before regex    |\n\n> All entries above are exercised by `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py`\n> (T01.05 unit surface, 66 assertions). Capability gating\n659\t\n660\t### Three tiers of gates\n661\t\n662\t| Tier | Trigger | Behavior |\n663\t|---|---|---|\n664\t| **HARD** | `which claude` returns nonzero | Abort run with exit code 2 + error msg. Cannot proceed. |\n665\t| **SOFT-SKIP** | User passed `--no-mcp` or `mcp_server.auggie` check fails | Mark gated evals as SKIPPED with reason. Continue. |\n666\t| **SOFT-XFAIL** | Manifest declares `xfail_if: <condition>` | Run, but expected-fail. Status is XPASS if it passes anyway. |\n667\t\n668\t### Capability check implementation (interface only)\n669\t\n670\t```python\n671\t# cli/eval/capability_gates.py\n672\t\n673\t@dataclass\n674\tclass Capability:\n675\t    name: str\n676\t    check: Callable[[], bool]\n677\t    failure_mode: Literal[\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"]\n678\t    skip_flag: Optional[str] = None    # CLI flag that forces skip (e.g., --no-mcp)\n679\t    description: str = \"\"\n680\t\n681\tCAPABILITIES = [\n682\t    Capability(\"binary.claude\",  lambda: shutil.which(\"claude\")  is not None, \"hard\"),\n683\t    Capability(\"binary.make\",    lambda: shutil.which(\"make\")    is not None, \"hard\"),\n684\t    Capability(\"binary.jq\",      lambda: shutil.which(\"jq\")      is not None, \"hard\"),\n685\t    Capability(\"binary.git\",     lambda: shutil.which(\"git\")     is not None, \"hard\"),\n686\t    Capability(\"mcp_server.auggie\",            mcp_server_reachable(\"auggie\"),            \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n687\t    Capability(\"mcp_server.auggie-mcp\",        mcp_server_reachable(\"auggie-mcp\"),        \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n688\t    Capability(\"mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway\", mcp_server_reachable(\"airis-mcp-gateway\"), \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n689\t]\n690\t\n691\tdef check_all(skip_flags: set[str]) -> CapabilityReport:\n692\t    \"\"\"Run all checks; return per-cap status + which evals are blocked.\"\"\"\n693\t```\n694\t\n695\t### `eval doctor` output\n696\t\n697\t```\n698\t🔧 superclaude eval doctor\n699\t\n700\tHard requirements:\n701\t  ✅ claude:  /usr/local/bin/claude (v0.5.3)\n702\t  ✅ make:    /usr/bin/make\n703\t  ✅ jq:      /usr/bin/jq\n704\t  ✅ git:     /usr/bin/git\n705\t\n706\tOptional capabilities:\n707\t  ✅ mcp_server.auggie:            reachable via stdio\n708\t  ✅ mcp_server.auggie-mcp:        reachable via stdio\n709\t  ❌ mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway: unreachable (1 eval will skip)\n710\t\n711\tVerdict: READY (1 eval will be skipped without --no-mcp override)\n712\t```\n713\t\n714\t---\n715\t\n716\t## 12. Concurrency model\n717\t\n718\t### Default: `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=8)`\n719\t\n720\tThe orchestrator uses `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (matching `cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`'s pattern, NOT the heavier `execution/parallel.py`). Each eval runs in its own thread; the thread blocks on `PtyDriver.wait()` which is itself a `pexpect` blocking read.\n721\t\n722\t### Isolation guarantees at high concurrency\n723\t\n724\t1. **No shared mutable state** — each eval has its own `HomeIsolation` with its own HOME dir.\n725\t2. **No shared file handles** — each eval opens its own `auggie-first.jsonl` under its HOME.\n726\t3. **No port collisions** — Claude Code TTY doesn't bind ports for the evals we run.\n727\t4. **Bounded process count** — at `--parallel 8`, max 8 `claude` subprocesses concurrently. At `--parallel 15`, 15. Each consumes ~150MB resident; 15-way needs ~2.25GB free.\n728\t\n729\t### Bounded retry (no infinite retry)\n730\t\n731\tFailed evals are NOT retried by default. The harness produces a deterministic single-pass run. The user can re-run with `--eval <failed-ids>` after diagnosing.\n732\t\n733\t### Signal handling\n734\t\n735\t- `SIGINT` (Ctrl-C): Cancel all running evals, mark in-flight as INTERRUPTED, write partial summary, exit 3.\n736\t- `SIGTERM`: Same as SIGINT.\n737\t- Per-eval timeout: kill the `PtyDriver` subprocess + reap zombie; mark eval as TIMEOUT (counts as FAIL).\n738\t\n739\t### What we DON'T do at high concurrency\n740\t\n741\t- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n742\t- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n743\t- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n744\t\n745\t---\n746\t\n747\t## 13. Fork strategy — vendored `ptytest`\n748\t\n749\t### What we fork"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"58eccff7-612f-4039-9bf8-9e1ce946da1f","uuid":"63b1b2c2-fdd9-463c-9b19-26327efe21db","timestamp":"2026-05-20T05:13:12.033Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/design-spec.md","content":"13 new files under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/` and `cli/eval/pty/` (vendored ptytest). See §3 directory layout.\n\n### Test coverage for the harness itself\n\n6 new test files under `tests/cli/test_eval/` (see §3). These are pytest-based and DO use mocks — the harness *itself* is the system under test; we mock the spawned subprocess for harness unit tests, then E1-E15 are the real-subprocess validation of the harness in production mode.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Capability gating\n\n### Three tiers of gates\n\n| Tier | Trigger | Behavior |\n|---|---|---|\n| **HARD** | `which claude` returns nonzero | Abort run with exit code 2 + error msg. Cannot proceed. |\n| **SOFT-SKIP** | User passed `--no-mcp` or `mcp_server.auggie` check fails | Mark gated evals as SKIPPED with reason. Continue. |\n| **SOFT-XFAIL** | Manifest declares `xfail_if: <condition>` | Run, but expected-fail. Status is XPASS if it passes anyway. |\n\n### Capability check implementation (interface only)\n\n```python\n# cli/eval/capability_gates.py\n\n@dataclass\nclass Capability:\n    name: str\n    check: Callable[[], bool]\n    failure_mode: Literal[\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"]\n    skip_flag: Optional[str] = None    # CLI flag that forces skip (e.g., --no-mcp)\n    description: str = \"\"\n\nCAPABILITIES = [\n    Capability(\"binary.claude\",  lambda: shutil.which(\"claude\")  is not None, \"hard\"),\n    Capability(\"binary.make\",    lambda: shutil.which(\"make\")    is not None, \"hard\"),\n    Capability(\"binary.jq\",      lambda: shutil.which(\"jq\")      is not None, \"hard\"),\n    Capability(\"binary.git\",     lambda: shutil.which(\"git\")     is not None, \"hard\"),\n    Capability(\"mcp_server.auggie\",            mcp_server_reachable(\"auggie\"),            \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n    Capability(\"mcp_server.auggie-mcp\",        mcp_server_reachable(\"auggie-mcp\"),        \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n    Capability(\"mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway\", mcp_server_reachable(\"airis-mcp-gateway\"), \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n]\n\ndef check_all(skip_flags: set[str]) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Run all checks; return per-cap status + which evals are blocked.\"\"\"\n```\n\n### `eval doctor` output\n\n```\n🔧 superclaude eval doctor\n\nHard requirements:\n  ✅ claude:  /usr/local/bin/claude (v0.5.3)\n  ✅ make:    /usr/bin/make\n  ✅ jq:      /usr/bin/jq\n  ✅ git:     /usr/bin/git\n\nOptional capabilities:\n  ✅ mcp_server.auggie:            reachable via stdio\n  ✅ mcp_server.auggie-mcp:        reachable via stdio\n  ❌ mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway: unreachable (1 eval will skip)\n\nVerdict: READY (1 eval will be skipped without --no-mcp override)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 12. Concurrency model\n\n### Default: `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=8)`\n\nThe orchestrator uses `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (matching `cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`'s pattern, NOT the heavier `execution/parallel.py`). Each eval runs in its own thread; the thread blocks on `PtyDriver.wait()` which is itself a `pexpect` blocking read.\n\n### Isolation guarantees at high concurrency\n\n1. **No shared mutable state** — each eval has its own `HomeIsolation` with its own HOME dir.\n2. **No shared file handles** — each eval opens its own `auggie-first.jsonl` under its HOME.\n3. **No port collisions** — Claude Code TTY doesn't bind ports for the evals we run.\n4. **Bounded process count** — at `--parallel 8`, max 8 `claude` subprocesses concurrently. At `--parallel 15`, 15. Each consumes ~150MB resident; 15-way needs ~2.25GB free.\n\n### Bounded retry (no infinite retry)\n\nFailed evals are NOT retried by default. The harness produces a deterministic single-pass run. The user can re-run with `--eval <failed-ids>` after diagnosing.\n\n### Signal handling\n\n- `SIGINT` (Ctrl-C): Cancel all running evals, mark in-flight as INTERRUPTED, write partial summary, exit 3.\n- `SIGTERM`: Same as SIGINT.\n- Per-eval timeout: kill the `PtyDriver` subprocess + reap zombie; mark eval as TIMEOUT (counts as FAIL).\n\n### What we DON'T do at high concurrency\n\n- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n\n---\n\n## 13. Fork strategy — vendored `ptytest`\n\n### What we fork","numLines":100,"startLine":650,"totalLines":844}}}

import annotations\n\nimport re\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Iterable, Mapping, Protocol, runtime_checkable\n\nimport yaml\nfrom jsonschema import Draft202012Validator\nfrom jsonschema.exceptions import ValidationError\n\nfrom .models import EvalSpec\nfrom .suites import SCHEMA_PATH\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"CapabilityResolver\",\n    \"EVAL_ID_REGEX\",\n    \"INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"InvalidEvalId\",\n    \"ParsedSuite\",\n    \"PermissiveCapabilityResolver\",\n    \"SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"SchemaError\",\n    \"SuiteLoader\",\n    \"SuiteLoaderError\",\n    \"UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"UnresolvedCapability\",\n    \"validate_eval_id\",\n    \"validate_manifest\",\n]","originalFile":"\"\"\"Manifest loader entry points for the cliEval suite pipeline.\n\nRoadmap FR-SCH1 / Deliverable D-0004 (Task T01.04).\n\nThis module exposes :func:`validate_manifest`, the single entry point used by\nboth ``eval doctor`` (T01.13) and ``eval run`` (M2 commands) to turn a\n``suites/*.yaml`` manifest into a list of :class:`~superclaude.cli.eval.models.EvalSpec`\nrecords *before any filesystem write occurs*.\n\nValidation pipeline (per design-spec §5 / FR-SCH1):\n\n1. Read the manifest from disk as raw bytes and decode YAML.\n2. Validate the decoded mapping against ``suite.schema.json`` (DM-011,\n   T01.02) using ``jsonschema``'s Draft 2020-12 validator.\n3. On any violation raise :class:`SchemaError` with a stable field-path\n   message; the CLI catches it and exits with :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`\n   (= 2 per the harness exit-code table in design-spec §4).\n4. On success, project every ``evals[]`` entry through\n   ``EvalSpec.from_dict()`` and return the resulting list.\n\nWhat this module is **not** responsible for:\n\n* FR-SCH2 eval-id regex enforcement — lives in :func:`validate_eval_id`\n  (T01.05) and is applied by the SuiteLoader orchestrator (T01.07) both\n  pre- and post-parameterize-expansion.\n* Capability resolution and parameterize expansion — also COMP-002 (T01.07).\n* Any filesystem write (artifact dirs, scratch HOMEs, log files).\n  Callers MUST treat ``validate_manifest`` as a pure read so that schema\n  rejections cannot leak partial state onto disk (NFR-SEC1 invariant).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Iterable, Mapping\n\nimport yaml\nfrom jsonschema import Draft202012Validator\nfrom jsonschema.exceptions import ValidationError\n\nfrom .models import EvalSpec\nfrom .suites import SCHEMA_PATH\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"EVAL_ID_REGEX\",\n    \"INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"InvalidEvalId\",\n    \"SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"SchemaError\",\n    \"validate_eval_id\",\n    \"validate_manifest\",\n]\n\n\nSCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`SchemaError` reaches the CLI boundary.\n\nMatches the design-spec §4 exit-code table: ``2`` = \"Harness error\n(manifest invalid, claude binary missing, etc.)\". The constant is exported\nso CLI callers and tests can assert against a single source of truth\nwithout duplicating the literal.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nINVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`InvalidEvalId` reaches the CLI boundary.\n\nFR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1 maps every malformed eval id to the same exit code as\n:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) because both are surfaces of the same\n\"harness rejected the manifest before any filesystem write\" outcome from\nthe operator's point of view. The constants are kept separate so call\nsites can branch on intent without coupling the two failure classes.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nEVAL_ID_REGEX: re.Pattern[str] = re.compile(\n    r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\"\n)\n\"\"\"Compiled FR-SCH2 eval-id regex (single source of truth for the runtime guard).\n\nMatches the literal regex declared in design-spec §5 and mirrored by the\n``evalIdString`` ``pattern`` inside ``suite.schema.json`` (T01.02). The\npattern accepts canonical eval ids (``E1``, ``E15``, ``D15``) and\nparameterize-expanded ids (``E2.1``, ``E2.10``) while rejecting:\n\n* Path-traversal patterns: ``../home``, ``/etc``, ``..``, ``./foo``.\n* Embedded separators: ``foo/bar``, ``foo\\\\bar``, ``E1/x``.\n* Empty strings and leading-digit ids (``1E``, ``9``).\n* Template tokens that leaked past parameterize expansion\n  (``{{prefix}}``, ``${var}``).\n\nThe pattern is compiled at module import so the loader hot path (one\n:func:`validate_eval_id` call per eval, plus one per parameterize-\nexpanded id) avoids re-parsing on every check.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nclass InvalidEvalId(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an eval id fails the FR-SCH2 regex.\n\n    The guard is applied in two places by COMP-002 ``SuiteLoader`` (T01.07):\n\n    1. At loader entry, against every static id in the manifest. (The\n       schema layer rejects malformed static ids too, but the runtime\n       guard is the authoritative check the security model relies on.)\n    2. After parameterize expansion, against every generated id (``E2.1``,\n       ``E2.2``, ...). This closes the path-traversal attack surface\n       where a parameterize substitution could otherwise inject ``..`` or\n       ``/`` into an id that then gets interpolated into\n       ``home_root / eval_id / home`` by ``HomeIsolation``.\n\n    The exception MUST raise before any filesystem write (NFR-SEC1\n    invariant) and the CLI MUST map it to :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`\n    (= 2).\n\n    Attributes:\n        eval_id: The exact value that failed the regex. Stored as the\n            original input (``repr``-rendered in :meth:`__str__`) so\n            reporters can show the offending payload verbatim — including\n            quotes around whitespace or empty values — without losing\n            forensic detail.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, eval_id: Any) -> None:\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"eval id failed FR-SCH2 regex ({EVAL_ID_REGEX.pattern!r}): \"\n            f\"{eval_id!r}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef validate_eval_id(eval_id: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Enforce the FR-SCH2 eval-id regex.\n\n    The function is a pure guard: it returns ``None`` on success and\n    raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` otherwise. No filesystem access, no\n    logging, no globals touched — so callers can apply it freely on\n    the hot path without worrying about side effects.\n\n    Args:\n        eval_id: The eval identifier to check. Schema-valid manifests\n            already constrain this to a string at the schema layer, but\n            the runtime guard does its own type check so callers that\n            bypass the schema (e.g., post-parameterize expansion code\n            paths) cannot accidentally smuggle non-string scalars\n            through.\n\n    Raises:\n        InvalidEvalId: Raised when ``eval_id`` is not a ``str`` or when\n            it does not match :data:`EVAL_ID_REGEX`. The exception\n            carries the offending value on :attr:`InvalidEvalId.eval_id`\n            and MUST be mapped by the CLI boundary to\n            :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Type guard first so YAML scalars that decode to int/bool/None cannot\n    # bypass the regex check via duck typing.\n    if not isinstance(eval_id, str):\n        raise InvalidEvalId(eval_id)\n\n    if EVAL_ID_REGEX.fullmatch(eval_id) is None:\n        raise InvalidEvalId(eval_id)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass _Violation:\n    \"\"\"One jsonschema error projected into a stable, log-friendly shape.\"\"\"\n\n    path: str\n    message: str\n\n    def render(self) -> str:\n        return f\"{self.path}: {self.message}\"\n\n\nclass SchemaError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a manifest fails ``suite.schema.json`` validation.\n\n    The exception carries the manifest path, every collected\n    :class:`jsonschema.exceptions.ValidationError` projected into a\n    field-path / message pair, and a pre-rendered multi-line ``message``\n    suitable for stderr. The CLI maps this to exit code\n    :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n    Attributes:\n        manifest_path: Filesystem path of the manifest that failed.\n        violations: Tuple of ``(json_path, error_message)`` pairs, one per\n            jsonschema error. Order is deterministic (sorted by JSON path)\n            so reporter output is stable across runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        manifest_path: Path,\n        violations: Iterable[_Violation],\n    ) -> None:\n        self.manifest_path = manifest_path\n        self.violations: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = tuple(\n            (v.path, v.message) for v in violations\n        )\n        lines = [f\"Manifest schema validation failed: {manifest_path}\"]\n        lines.extend(f\"  - {path}: {msg}\" for path, msg in self.violations)\n        super().__init__(\"\\n\".join(lines))\n\n\ndef _format_json_path(error: ValidationError) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a jsonschema error path as ``$.evals[0].id`` notation.\n\n    ``$`` denotes the manifest root so the top-level key for a missing\n    required field is also reportable (jsonschema leaves ``absolute_path``\n    empty in that case and stores the field name in ``message``).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not error.absolute_path:\n        return \"$\"\n    parts: list[str] = [\"$\"]\n    for segment in error.absolute_path:\n        if isinstance(segment, int):\n            parts.append(f\"[{segment}]\")\n        else:\n            parts.append(f\".{segment}\")\n    return \"\".join(parts)\n\n\ndef _load_schema() -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    return yaml.safe_load(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n\ndef _read_manifest(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Decode the YAML manifest at ``path``.\n\n    The read is a pure ``open(...).read()`` — no scratch directories or\n    artifact paths are touched. YAML decode errors surface as\n    :class:`SchemaError` so CLI callers only have to handle a single\n    typed error to honour FR-SCH1.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        raw = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest file not found: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n    except OSError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest read failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    try:\n        decoded = yaml.safe_load(raw)\n    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"YAML decode failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    if not isinstance(decoded, Mapping):\n        violation = _Violation(\n            path=\"$\",\n            message=(\n                \"manifest must decode to a mapping at the top level; \"\n                f\"got {type(decoded).__name__}\"\n            ),\n        )\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,))\n\n    return decoded\n\n\ndef validate_manifest(path: Path | str) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n    \"\"\"Validate ``path`` against ``suite.schema.json`` and return parsed evals.\n\n    Args:\n        path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str`` is\n            accepted for ergonomics; the value is normalised to ``Path``.\n\n    Returns:\n        A list of :class:`EvalSpec` instances, one per ``evals[]`` entry\n        in the manifest. The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. The\n    real implementation arriving in T01.11 inspects PATH + MCP reachability\n    + ``--no-mcp`` flags and either returns the set of unresolved\n    capability names (which the loader turns into\n    :class:`UnresolvedCapability`) or returns an empty iterable.\n\n    Test code injects a stub that records its calls so the \"id-check\n    happens BEFORE capability resolution\" acceptance criterion can be\n    verified by call-order assertion rather than by side-effects.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the subset of ``required`` that could not be satisfied.\n\n        Implementations MUST be pure with respect to the SuiteLoader\n        invariants: no filesystem writes, no globals touched. Returning\n        an empty iterable means every capability is satisfied; any\n        non-empty iterable is bubbled up by the loader as\n        :class:`UnresolvedCapability`.\n        \"\"\"\n        ...\n\n\nclass PermissiveCapabilityResolver:\n    \"\"\"Default :class:`CapabilityResolver` that approves every requirement.\n\n    Used by :class:`SuiteLoader` when the caller does not inject a real\n    resolver — tests and ``eval doctor``'s dry runs both benefit from a\n    no-op default. The real ``CapabilityGates`` implementation lands in\n    T01.11 and is wired through ``commands.py`` (T01.13).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        return ()\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ParsedSuite:\n    \"\"\"Schema-validated, capability-checked, parameterize-expanded suite.\n\n    The :class:`SuiteLoader.load` return shape. Every field is a verbatim\n    projection of the manifest envelope except ``evals``, which holds the\n    *expanded* eval list (parameterize rows materialised into one\n    ``EvalSpec`` per row, with ``.{index}``-suffixed ids).\n\n    Sequence fields are tuples so the dataclass is hashable and downstream\n    consumers cannot mutate them through the container.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    description: str\n    defaults: Mapping[str, Any]\n    required_binaries: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    optional_capabilities: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    evals: tuple[EvalSpec, ...]\n    source_path: Path\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass SuiteLoader:\n    \"\"\"COMP-002 orchestrator: turn a manifest path into a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n    The gate ordering is fixed and documented in\n    ``artifacts/D-0006/spec.md`` (T01.07):\n\n    1. **Read + schema** (``_validate_manifest_dict``) — FR-SCH1.\n    2. **Static id regex** (``validate_eval_id`` on every ``evals[].id``) —\n       FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1. Runs BEFORE capability resolution so a\n       traversal-pattern id cannot trigger ``shutil.which`` calls or\n       network MCP-reachability probes.\n    3. **Capability resolution** (the injected :class:`CapabilityResolver`)\n       — COMP-009 contract surface.\n    4. **Parameterize expansion** — generate ``E2.1, E2.2, ...`` from\n       ``parameterize:`` rows, base-id suffixed by ``.{index}``.\n    5. **Expanded-id regex re-check** (``validate_eval_id`` again) —\n       the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application that closes the\n       expansion-time path-traversal surface.\n\n    Every typed error (``SchemaError``, ``InvalidEvalId``,\n    ``UnresolvedCapability``) is allowed to propagate as-is so CLI\n    callers map it to :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) and\n    emit the error class name on stderr — operators see *which* gate\n    fired without us collapsing the failure classes.\n\n    The class is intentionally a plain dataclass with one composed\n    field (the resolver) rather than a long ``__init__`` parameter list,\n    so future T01.14 (ExpectDSL) wiring can be added as additional\n    optional fields without breaking call sites.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    capability_resolver: CapabilityResolver = field(\n        default_factory=PermissiveCapabilityResolver\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # public API\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def load(self, path: Path | str) -> ParsedSuite:\n        \"\"\"Run the five-stage gate chain and return a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n        Args:\n            path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str``\n                accepted for ergonomics; normalised to ``Path``.\n\n        Returns:\n            A :class:`ParsedSuite` whose ``evals`` tuple holds the\n            post-parameterize-expansion eval list. Suite-level metadata\n            is verbatim from the manifest envelope so callers do not\n            need to re-read the file to access ``required_binaries``,\n            ``defaults``, etc.\n\n        Raises:\n            SchemaError: Manifest read / YAML decode / jsonschema\n                violation. The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. The","+    real implementation arriving in T01.11 inspects PATH + MCP reachability","+    + ``--no-mcp`` flags and either returns the set of unresolved","+    capability names (which the loader turns into","+    :class:`UnresolvedCapability`) or returns an empty iterable.","+","+    Test code injects a stub that records its calls so the \"id-check","+    happens BEFORE capability resolution\" acceptance criterion can be","+    verified by call-order assertion rather than by side-effects.","+    \"\"\"","+","+    def resolve(","+        self,","+        eval_id: str,","+        required: tuple[str, ...],","+    ) -> Iterable[str]:","+        \"\"\"Return the subset of ``required`` that could not be satisfied.","+","+        Implementations MUST be pure with respect to the SuiteLoader","+        invariants: no filesystem writes, no globals touched. Returning","+        an empty iterable means every capability is satisfied; any","+        non-empty iterable is bubbled up by the loader as","+        :class:`UnresolvedCapability`.","+        \"\"\"","+        ...","+","+","+class PermissiveCapabilityResolver:","+    \"\"\"Default :class:`CapabilityResolver` that approves every requirement.","+","+    Used by :class:`SuiteLoader` when the caller does not inject a real","+    resolver — tests and ``eval doctor``'s dry runs both benefit from a","+    no-op default. The function accepts\n17\t  any value at runtime so non-string scalars (`int`, `bool`, `None`,\n18\t  `bytes`) that may leak through YAML decoding are rejected via the\n19\t  type guard rather than slipping past the regex check.\n20\t- **Output:** `None` on success (the function is a pure guard).\n21\t- **Error behaviour:** raises `InvalidEvalId` carrying the offending\n22\t  value on `InvalidEvalId.eval_id`. CLI callers MUST map this to\n23\t  `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n24\t\n25\t## The regex\n26\t\n27\t```python\n28\tEVAL_ID_REGEX = re.compile(r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\")\n29\t```\n30\t\n31\tThis is the single source of truth for the runtime guard. The same\n32\tregex literal is encoded into `suites/suite.schema.json` (T01.02) as\n33\tthe `evalIdString` `pattern`, so schema validation rejects unsafe\n34\t**static** ids at the schema layer too; `validate_eval_id` is the\n35\tauthoritative runtime check the security model relies on (especially\n36\tfor post-parameterize-expanded ids that never touch the schema).\n37\t\n38\tThe pattern accepts:\n39\t\n40\t| Shape           | Examples                          |\n41\t|-----------------|-----------------------------------|\n42\t| Canonical eval  | `E1`, `E2`, `E15`, `D15`          |\n43\t| Parameter-ized  | `E2.1`, `E2.2`, `E2.10`, `Foo42.7`|\n44\t| Single letter   | `A`, `Z9`                         |\n45\t| Mixed-case body | `Test1`, `ABC123`                 |\n46\t\n47\tIt rejects everything else; the named negative cases below are the\n48\tfloor, not the ceiling.\n49\t\n50\t## Negative-case checklist (FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1)\n51\t\n52\t| Category                  | Examples                                                 | Why it must fail                                              |\n53\t|---------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|\n54\t| Empty string              | `\"\"`                                                     | AC bullet; trivially escapes `home_root / \"\" / \"home\"`        |\n55\t| Path-traversal prefix     | `../home`, `../../etc`, `..`                             | AC bullet; escapes the scratch root via `Path` join semantics |\n56\t| Absolute path             | `/etc`, `/tmp/eval-runs`                                 | AC bullet; `Path(\"/abs\") / \"home\"` discards `home_root`       |\n57\t| Embedded separator        | `foo/bar`, `foo\\\\bar`, `E1/x`, `./foo`, `.`              | Lets attacker carve sub-directories under `home_root`         |\n58\t| Leading-digit id          | `1E`, `9`, `0E`, `1`, `12.3`                             | AC bullet; the schema also bans this — runtime mirrors        |\n59\t| Template token leakage    | `{{prefix}}`, `E{{p}}`, `${var}`, `<id>`, `%name%`       | AC bullet; an un-substituted token must NEVER reach the FS    |\n60\t| Whitespace / control char | ` E1`, `E1 `, `E 1`, `\\tE1`, `E1\\n`, `E1\\x00`            | Defence in depth — control chars confuse downstream consumers |\n61\t| Wrong character class     | `e1`, `eval1`, `E-1`, `E_1`                              | Underscores and lowercase are excluded by the regex anchor    |\n62\t| Malformed decimal         | `E1.`, `E.1`, `E1..1`, `E1.1.1`, `E1.1.`                 | Only one `.N` decimal allowed (mirrors parameterize semantics)|\n63\t| Non-string scalar         | `None`, `1`, `1.0`, `b\"E1\"`, `[\"E1\"]`, `(\"E1\",)`, `{...}`| YAML may decode bare scalars; type guard runs before regex    |\n64\t\n65\t> All entries above are exercised by `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py`\n66\t> (T01.05 unit surface, 66 assertions). The dedicated path-traversal\n67\t> negative-case test set (T01.08 / NFR-SEC1) cross-links the same\n68\t> categories at the integration layer.\n69\t\n70\t## Error → exit-code mapping\n71\t\n72\t| Trigger                                      | Raised           | Exit code                  |\n73\t|----------------------------------------------|------------------|----------------------------|\n74\t| `eval_id` is not `str`                       | `InvalidEvalId`  | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`|\n75\t| `eval_id` does not match `EVAL_ID_REGEX`     | `InvalidEvalId`  | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`|\n76\t\n77\t`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE = 2` is kept as its own constant (even though\n78\tthe value equals `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`) so call sites can branch on\n79\tthe *intent* of the rejection without coupling the two failure classes\n80\ttogether. Both surface to operators as the same \"harness rejected the\n81\tmanifest before any filesystem write\" outcome (design-spec §4).\n82\t\n83\t## Application sites (FR-SCH2 ordering)\n84\t\n85\t`validate_eval_id` is invoked in two places by the SuiteLoader\n86\torchestrator (T01.07 / COMP-002):\n87\t\n88\t1. **At loader entry**, against every static `evals[].id` in the parsed\n89\t   manifest. This is redundant with the schema layer for well-formed\n90\t   manifests but the security model treats the runtime guard as\n91\t   authoritative.\n92\t2. **After parameterize expansion**, against every generated id\n93\t   (`E2.1`, `E2.2`, ...). This is the *load-bearing* call — it closes\n94\t   the path-traversal attack surface where a malicious parameterize\n95\t   row could otherwise inject `..` or `/` into an id that\n96\t   `HomeIsolation` interpolates into `home_root / eval_id / home`.\n97\t\n98\tBoth call sites MUST run **before any filesystem write** (NFR-SEC1\n99\tinvariant). The unit tests in this task simulate the post-expansion\n100\tguard call directly; the actual SuiteLoader wiring lands in T01.07 and\n101\tits integration test verifies the no-FS-write contract via the same\n102\t`/tmp/eval-runs` snapshot pattern that `validate_manifest` already\n103\thonours (`test_rejection_does_not_write_to_default_scratch_root`).\n104\t\n105\t## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n106\t\n107\t| AC bullet (T01.05) | Implementation site |\n108\t|---|---|\n109\t| `validate_eval_id()` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/loader.py` raises `InvalidEvalId` for inputs `../home`, `/etc`, `..`, empty string, leading-digit IDs, and template tokens inside id. | `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py::test_validate_eval_id_rejects_traversal_and_separator_patterns`, `test_validate_eval_id_rejects_empty_string`, `test_validate_eval_id_rejects_leading_digit_ids`, `test_validate_eval_id_rejects_template_token_patterns`. |\n110\t| Guard is applied at SuiteLoader entry AND after parameterize expansion. | Unit-level surface here (`test_validate_eval_id_rejects_unsafe_expanded_id`, `test_validate_eval_id_accepts_safe_expanded_id`, `test_validate_eval_id_rejects_traversal_after_substitution`); SuiteLoader wiring + integration assertion lands in T01.07. |\n111\t| `InvalidEvalId` propagates to process exit code 2 via the loader error mapping. | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE = 2` exported from the loader; asserted by `test_invalid_eval_id_exit_code_is_two`. |\n112\t| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0005/spec.md` documents the regex and all negative cases. | This document (see \"The regex\" + \"Negative-case checklist\" sections). |\n113\t\n114\t## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n115\t\n116\t- **COMP-002 `SuiteLoader` (T01.07)** — calls `validate_eval_id` twice\n117\t  per eval (entry + post-expansion) as the authoritative runtime guard.\n118\t  Maps `InvalidEvalId` to `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`.\n119\t- **`HomeIsolation` (Phase 2)** — MAY re-apply the guard as a\n120\t  belt-and-braces check immediately before `home_root / eval_id /\n121\t  \"home\"` is constructed. This is defensive: the SuiteLoader should\n122\t  already have rejected the id, but `HomeIsolation` is the last layer\n123\t  before an actual FS write so the cost of the extra check (one\n124\t  compiled regex match) is negligible relative to the failure mode.\n125\t\n126\t## Notes / deferred work\n127\t\n128\t- `EVAL_ID_REGEX` is the **runtime** copy of the FR-SCH2 regex. The function accepts\n  any value at runtime so non-string scalars (`int`, `bool`, `None`,\n  `bytes`) that may leak through YAML decoding are rejected via the\n  type guard rather than slipping past the regex check.\n- **Output:** `None` on success (the function is a pure guard).\n- **Error behaviour:** raises `InvalidEvalId` carrying the offending\n  value on `InvalidEvalId.eval_id`. CLI callers MUST map this to\n  `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n## The regex\n\n```python\nEVAL_ID_REGEX = re.compile(r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\")\n```\n\nThis is the single source of truth for the runtime guard. The same\nregex literal is encoded into `suites/suite.schema.json` (T01.02) as\nthe `evalIdString` `pattern`, so schema validation rejects unsafe\n**static** ids at the schema layer too; `validate_eval_id` is the\nauthoritative runtime check the security model relies on (especially\nfor post-parameterize-expanded ids that never touch the schema).\n\nThe pattern accepts:\n\n| Shape           | Examples                          |\n|-----------------|-----------------------------------|\n| Canonical eval  | `E1`, `E2`, `E15`, `D15`          |\n| Parameter-ized  | `E2.1`, `E2.2`, `E2.10`, `Foo42.7`|\n| Single letter   | `A`, `Z9`                         |\n| Mixed-case body | `Test1`, `ABC123`                 |\n\nIt rejects everything else; the named negative cases below are the\nfloor, not the ceiling.\n\n## Negative-case checklist (FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1)\n\n| Category                  | Examples                                                 | Why it must fail                                              |\n|---------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|\n| Empty string              | `\"\"`                                                     | AC bullet; trivially escapes `home_root / \"\" / \"home\"`        |\n| Path-traversal prefix     | `../home`, `../../etc`, `..`                             | AC bullet; escapes the scratch root via `Path` join semantics |\n| Absolute path             | `/etc`, `/tmp/eval-runs`                                 | AC bullet; `Path(\"/abs\") / \"home\"` discards `home_root`       |\n| Embedded separator        | `foo/bar`, `foo\\\\bar`, `E1/x`, `./foo`, `.`              | Lets attacker carve sub-directories under `home_root`         |\n| Leading-digit id          | `1E`, `9`, `0E`, `1`, `12.3`                             | AC bullet; the schema also bans this — runtime mirrors        |\n| Template token leakage    | `{{prefix}}`, `E{{p}}`, `${var}`, `<id>`, `%name%`       | AC bullet; an un-substituted token must NEVER reach the FS    |\n| Whitespace / control char | ` E1`, `E1 `, `E 1`, `\\tE1`, `E1\\n`, `E1\\x00`            | Defence in depth — control chars confuse downstream consumers |\n| Wrong character class     | `e1`, `eval1`, `E-1`, `E_1`                              | Underscores and lowercase are excluded by the regex anchor    |\n| Malformed decimal         | `E1.`, `E.1`, `E1..1`, `E1.1.1`, `E1.1.`                 | Only one `.N` decimal allowed (mirrors parameterize semantics)|\n| Non-string scalar         | `None`, `1`, `1.0`, `b\"E1\"`, `[\"E1\"]`, `(\"E1\",)`, `{...}`| YAML may decode bare scalars; type guard runs before regex    |\n\n> All entries above are exercised by `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py`\n> (T01.05 unit surface, 66 assertions). The dedicated path-traversal\n> negative-case test set (T01.08 / NFR-SEC1) cross-links the same\n> categories at the integration layer.\n\n## Error → exit-code mapping\n\n| Trigger                                      | Raised           | Exit code                  |\n|----------------------------------------------|------------------|----------------------------|\n| `eval_id` is not `str`                       | `InvalidEvalId`  | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`|\n| `eval_id` does not match `EVAL_ID_REGEX`     | `InvalidEvalId`  | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`|\n\n`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE = 2` is kept as its own constant (even though\nthe value equals `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`) so call sites can branch on\nthe *intent* of the rejection without coupling the two failure classes\ntogether. Both surface to operators as the same \"harness rejected the\nmanifest before any filesystem write\" outcome (design-spec §4).\n\n## Application sites (FR-SCH2 ordering)\n\n`validate_eval_id` is invoked in two places by the SuiteLoader\norchestrator (T01.07 / COMP-002):\n\n1. **At loader entry**, against every static `evals[].id` in the parsed\n   manifest. This is redundant with the schema layer for well-formed\n   manifests but the security model treats the runtime guard as\n   authoritative.\n2. **After parameterize expansion**, against every generated id\n   (`E2.1`, `E2.2`, ...). This is the *load-bearing* call — it closes\n   the path-traversal attack surface where a malicious parameterize\n   row could otherwise inject `..` or `/` into an id that\n   `HomeIsolation` interpolates into `home_root / eval_id / home`.\n\nBoth call sites MUST run **before any filesystem write** (NFR-SEC1\ninvariant). The unit tests in this task simulate the post-expansion\nguard call directly; the actual SuiteLoader wiring lands in T01.07 and\nits integration test verifies the no-FS-write contract via the same\n`/tmp/eval-runs` snapshot pattern that `validate_manifest` already\nhonours (`test_rejection_does_not_write_to_default_scratch_root`).\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.05) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| `validate_eval_id()` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/loader.py` raises `InvalidEvalId` for inputs `../home`, `/etc`, `..`, empty string, leading-digit IDs, and template tokens inside id. | `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py::test_validate_eval_id_rejects_traversal_and_separator_patterns`, `test_validate_eval_id_rejects_empty_string`, `test_validate_eval_id_rejects_leading_digit_ids`, `test_validate_eval_id_rejects_template_token_patterns`. |\n| Guard is applied at SuiteLoader entry AND after parameterize expansion. | Unit-level surface here (`test_validate_eval_id_rejects_unsafe_expanded_id`, `test_validate_eval_id_accepts_safe_expanded_id`, `test_validate_eval_id_rejects_traversal_after_substitution`); SuiteLoader wiring + integration assertion lands in T01.07. |\n| `InvalidEvalId` propagates to process exit code 2 via the loader error mapping. | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE = 2` exported from the loader; asserted by `test_invalid_eval_id_exit_code_is_two`. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0005/spec.md` documents the regex and all negative cases. | This document (see \"The regex\" + \"Negative-case checklist\" sections). |\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n- **COMP-002 `SuiteLoader` (T01.07)** — calls `validate_eval_id` twice\n  per eval (entry + post-expansion) as the authoritative runtime guard.\n  Maps `InvalidEvalId` to `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`.\n- **`HomeIsolation` (Phase 2)** — MAY re-apply the guard as a\n  belt-and-braces check immediately before `home_root / eval_id /\n  \"home\"` is constructed. On success, project every ``evals[]`` entry through\n19\t   ``EvalSpec.from_dict()`` and return the resulting list.\n20\t\n21\tWhat this module is **not** responsible for:\n22\t\n23\t* FR-SCH2 eval-id regex enforcement — lives in :func:`validate_eval_id`\n24\t  (T01.05) and is applied by the SuiteLoader orchestrator (T01.07) both\n25\t  pre- and post-parameterize-expansion.\n26\t* Capability resolution and parameterize expansion — also COMP-002 (T01.07).\n27\t* Any filesystem write (artifact dirs, scratch HOMEs, log files).\n28\t  Callers MUST treat ``validate_manifest`` as a pure read so that schema\n29\t  rejections cannot leak partial state onto disk (NFR-SEC1 invariant).\n30\t\"\"\"\n31\t\n32\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n33\t\n34\timport re\n35\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n36\tfrom pathlib import Path\n37\tfrom typing import Any, Iterable, Mapping, Protocol, runtime_checkable\n38\t\n39\timport yaml\n40\tfrom jsonschema import Draft202012Validator\n41\tfrom jsonschema.exceptions import ValidationError\n42\t\n43\tfrom .models import EvalSpec\n44\tfrom .suites import SCHEMA_PATH\n45\t\n46\t__all__ = [\n47\t    \"CapabilityResolver\",\n48\t    \"EVAL_ID_REGEX\",\n49\t    \"INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE\",\n50\t    \"InvalidEvalId\",\n51\t    \"ParsedSuite\",\n52\t    \"PermissiveCapabilityResolver\",\n53\t    \"SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE\",\n54\t    \"SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE\",\n55\t    \"SchemaError\",\n56\t    \"SuiteLoader\",\n57\t    \"SuiteLoaderError\",\n58\t    \"UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE\",\n59\t    \"UnresolvedCapability\",\n60\t    \"validate_eval_id\",\n61\t    \"validate_manifest\",\n62\t]\n63\t\n64\t\n65\tSCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n66\t\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`SchemaError` reaches the CLI boundary.\n67\t\n68\tMatches the design-spec §4 exit-code table: ``2`` = \"Harness error\n69\t(manifest invalid, claude binary missing, etc.)\". The constant is exported\n70\tso CLI callers and tests can assert against a single source of truth\n71\twithout duplicating the literal.\n72\t\"\"\"\n73\t\n74\t\n75\tINVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n76\t\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`InvalidEvalId` reaches the CLI boundary.\n77\t\n78\tFR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1 maps every malformed eval id to the same exit code as\n79\t:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) because both are surfaces of the same\n80\t\"harness rejected the manifest before any filesystem write\" outcome from\n81\tthe operator's point of view. The constants are kept separate so call\n82\tsites can branch on intent without coupling the two failure classes.\n83\t\"\"\"\n84\t\n85\t\n86\tEVAL_ID_REGEX: re.Pattern[str] = re.compile(\n87\t    r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\"\n88\t)\n89\t\"\"\"Compiled FR-SCH2 eval-id regex (single source of truth for the runtime guard).\n90\t\n91\tMatches the literal regex declared in design-spec §5 and mirrored by the\n92\t``evalIdString`` ``pattern`` inside ``suite.schema.json`` (T01.02). The\n93\tpattern accepts canonical eval ids (``E1``, ``E15``, ``D15``) and\n94\tparameterize-expanded ids (``E2.1``, ``E2.10``) while rejecting:\n95\t\n96\t* Path-traversal patterns: ``../home``, ``/etc``, ``..``, ``./foo``.\n97\t* Embedded separators: ``foo/bar``, ``foo\\\\bar``, ``E1/x``.\n98\t* Empty strings and leading-digit ids (``1E``, ``9``).\n99\t* Template tokens that leaked past parameterize expansion\n100\t  (``{{prefix}}``, ``${var}``).\n101\t\n102\tThe pattern is compiled at module import so the loader hot path (one\n103\t:func:`validate_eval_id` call per eval, plus one per parameterize-\n104\texpanded id) avoids re-parsing on every check.\n105\t\"\"\"\n106\t\n107\t\n108\tclass InvalidEvalId(Exception):\n109\t    \"\"\"Raised when an eval id fails the FR-SCH2 regex.\n110\t\n111\t    The guard is applied in two places by COMP-002 ``SuiteLoader`` (T01.07):\n112\t\n113\t    1. At loader entry, against every static id in the manifest. (The\n114\t       schema layer rejects malformed static ids too, but the runtime\n115\t       guard is the authoritative check the security model relies on.)\n116\t    2. After parameterize expansion, against every generated id (``E2.1``,\n117\t       ``E2.2``, ...). This closes the path-traversal attack surface\n118\t       where a parameterize substitution could otherwise inject ``..`` or\n119\t       ``/`` into an id that then gets interpolated into\n120\t       ``home_root / eval_id / home`` by ``HomeIsolation``.\n121\t\n122\t    The exception MUST raise before any filesystem write (NFR-SEC1\n123\t    invariant) and the CLI MUST map it to :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`\n124\t    (= 2).\n125\t\n126\t    Attributes:\n127\t        eval_id: The exact value that failed the regex. Stored as the\n128\t            original input (``repr``-rendered in :meth:`__str__`) so\n129\t            reporters can show the offending payload verbatim — including\n130\t            quotes around whitespace or empty values — without losing\n131\t            forensic detail.\n132\t    \"\"\"\n133\t\n134\t    def __init__(self, eval_id: Any) -> None:\n135\t        self.eval_id = eval_id\n136\t        super().__init__(\n137\t            f\"eval id failed FR-SCH2 regex ({EVAL_ID_REGEX.pattern!r}): \"\n138\t            f\"{eval_id!r}\"\n139\t        )\n140\t\n141\t\n142\tdef validate_eval_id(eval_id: str) -> None:\n143\t    \"\"\"Enforce the FR-SCH2 eval-id regex.\n144\t\n145\t    The function is a pure guard: it returns ``None`` on success and\n146\t    raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` otherwise. No filesystem access, no\n147\t    logging, no globals touched — so callers can apply it freely on\n148\t    the hot path without worrying about side effects.\n149\t\n150\t    Args:\n151\t        eval_id: The eval identifier to check. Schema-valid manifests\n152\t            already constrain this to a string at the schema layer, but\n153\t            the runtime guard does its own type check so callers that\n154\t            bypass the schema (e.g., post-parameterize expansion code\n155\t            paths) cannot accidentally smuggle non-string scalars\n156\t            through.\n157\t\n158\t    Raises:\n159\t        InvalidEvalId: Raised when ``eval_id`` is not a ``str`` or when\n160\t            it does not match :data:`EVAL_ID_REGEX`. The exception\n161\t            carries the offending value on :attr:`InvalidEvalId.eval_id`\n162\t            and MUST be mapped by the CLI boundary to\n163\t            :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n164\t    \"\"\"\n165\t\n166\t    # Type guard first so YAML scalars that decode to int/bool/None cannot\n167\t    # bypass the regex check via duck typing.\n168\t    if not isinstance(eval_id, str):\n169\t        raise InvalidEvalId(eval_id)\n170\t\n171\t    if EVAL_ID_REGEX.fullmatch(eval_id) is None:\n172\t        raise InvalidEvalId(eval_id)\n173\t\n174\t\n175\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n176\tclass _Violation:\n177\t    \"\"\"One jsonschema error projected into a stable, log-friendly shape.\"\"\"\n178\t\n179\t    path: str\n180\t    message: str\n181\t\n182\t    def render(self) -> str:\n183\t        return f\"{self.path}: {self.message}\"\n184\t\n185\t\n186\tclass SchemaError(Exception):\n187\t    \"\"\"Raised when a manifest fails ``suite.schema.json`` validation.\n188\t\n189\t    The exception carries the manifest path, every collected\n190\t    :class:`jsonschema.exceptions.ValidationError` projected into a\n191\t    field-path / message pair, and a pre-rendered multi-line ``message``\n192\t    suitable for stderr. The CLI maps this to exit code\n193\t    :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n194\t\n195\t    Attributes:\n196\t        manifest_path: Filesystem path of the manifest that failed.\n197\t        violations: Tuple of ``(json_path, error_message)`` pairs, one per\n198\t            jsonschema error. Order is deterministic (sorted by JSON path)\n199\t            so reporter output is stable across runs.\n200\t    \"\"\"\n201\t\n202\t    def __init__(\n203\t        self,\n204\t        manifest_path: Path,\n205\t        violations: Iterable[_Violation],\n206\t    ) -> None:\n207\t        self.manifest_path = manifest_path\n208\t        self.violations: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = tuple(\n209\t            (v.path, v.message) for v in violations\n210\t        )\n211\t        lines = [f\"Manifest schema validation failed: {manifest_path}\"]\n212\t        lines.extend(f\"  - {path}: {msg}\" for path, msg in self.violations)\n213\t        super().__init__(\"\\n\".join(lines))\n214\t\n215\t\n216\tdef _format_json_path(error: ValidationError) -> str:\n217\t    \"\"\"Render a jsonschema error path as ``$.evals[0].id`` notation.\n218\t\n219\t    ``$`` denotes the manifest root so the top-level key for a missing\n220\t    required field is also reportable (jsonschema leaves ``absolute_path``\n221\t    empty in that case and stores the field name in ``message``).\n222\t    \"\"\"\n223\t\n224\t    if not error.absolute_path:\n225\t        return \"$\"\n226\t    parts: list[str] = [\"$\"]\n227\t    for segment in error.absolute_path:\n228\t        if isinstance(segment, int):\n229\t            parts.append(f\"[{segment}]\")\n230\t        else:\n231\t            parts.append(f\".{segment}\")\n232\t    return \"\".join(parts)\n233\t\n234\t\n235\tdef _load_schema() -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n236\t    return yaml.safe_load(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n237\t\n238\t\n239\tdef _read_manifest(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n240\t    \"\"\"Decode the YAML manifest at ``path``.\n241\t\n242\t    The read is a pure ``open(...).read()`` — no scratch directories or\n243\t    artifact paths are touched. YAML decode errors surface as\n244\t    :class:`SchemaError` so CLI callers only have to handle a single\n245\t    typed error to honour FR-SCH1.\n246\t    \"\"\"\n247\t\n248\t    try:\n249\t        raw = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n250\t    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n251\t        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest file not found: {exc}\")\n252\t        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n253\t    except OSError as exc:\n254\t        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest read failed: {exc}\")\n255\t        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n256\t\n257\t    try:\n258\t        decoded = yaml.safe_load(raw)\n259\t    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:\n260\t        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"YAML decode failed: {exc}\")\n261\t        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n262\t\n263\t    if not isinstance(decoded, Mapping):\n264\t        violation = _Violation(\n265\t            path=\"$\",\n266\t            message=(\n267\t                \"manifest must decode to a mapping at the top level; \"\n268\t                f\"got {type(decoded).__name__}\"\n269\t            ),\n270\t        )\n271\t        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,))\n272\t\n273\t    return decoded\n274\t\n275\t\n276\tdef _validate_manifest_dict(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n277\t    \"\"\"Read ``path``, decode YAML, and validate against ``suite.schema.json``.\n278\t\n279\t    Returns the raw manifest mapping (suite envelope + ``evals[]``) so\n280\t    callers that need suite-level metadata (``name``, ``version``,\n281\t    ``required_binaries``, etc.) — notably :class:`SuiteLoader` — do not\n282\t    have to re-read or re-parse the file. This is the single ingress\n283\t    point for FR-SCH1: every schema rejection raised inside the loader\n284\t    flows through here.\n285\t    \"\"\"\n286\t\n287\t    manifest = _read_manifest(path)\n288\t\n289\t    schema = _load_schema()\n290\t    validator = Draft202012Validator(schema)\n291\t    errors = sorted(\n292\t        validator.iter_errors(manifest),\n293\t        key=lambda e: (list(e.absolute_path), e.message),\n294\t    )\n295\t    if errors:\n296\t        violations = [\n297\t            _Violation(path=_format_json_path(err), message=err.message)\n298\t            for err in errors\n299\t        ]\n300\t        raise SchemaError(path, violations)\n301\t\n302\t    return manifest\n303\t\n304\t\n305\tdef validate_manifest(path: Path | str) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n306\t    \"\"\"Validate ``path`` against ``suite.schema.json`` and return parsed evals.\n307\t\n308\t    Args:\n309\t        path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str`` is\n310\t            accepted for ergonomics; the value is normalised to ``Path``.\n311\t\n312\t    Returns:\n313\t        A list of :class:`EvalSpec` instances, one per ``evals[]`` entry\n314\t        in the manifest. Order matches the manifest order so downstream\n315\t        report ordering is deterministic.\n316\t\n317\t    Raises:\n318\t        SchemaError: Raised when the manifest cannot be read, cannot be\n319\t            decoded as YAML, decodes to a non-mapping, or violates any\n320\t            constraint in ``suite.schema.json``.
