CLI callers MUST map\n323\t            this to :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n324\t    \"\"\"\n325\t\n326\t    manifest_path = Path(path)\n327\t    manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n328\t    return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry) for entry in manifest[\"evals\"]]\n329\t\n330\t\n331\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n332\t# COMP-002 SuiteLoader (T01.07 / D-0006)\n333\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n334\t\n335\t\n336\tUNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n337\t\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`UnresolvedCapability` reaches the CLI.\n338\t\n339\tThe third member of the SuiteLoader error trio (alongside\n340\t:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` and :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`). All\n341\tthree map to ``2`` per the design-spec §4 exit-code table so operators see\n342\ta single \"harness rejected the manifest before any filesystem write\"\n343\toutcome regardless of which gate fired.\n344\t\"\"\"\n345\t\n346\t\n347\tSUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n348\t\"\"\"Single canonical exit code for every :class:`SuiteLoaderError` subclass.\n349\t\n350\t``SuiteLoader`` only ever exits with ``2`` because every loader-layer\n351\tfailure is, from the operator's point of view, \"the harness refused to\n352\tstart running the suite\". The per-class constants\n353\t(:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`,\n354\t:data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`) are kept so callers can branch on\n355\tintent without coupling the failure classes; this constant is the\n356\taggregate ``SuiteLoader``-boundary alias used by CLI tests.\n357\t\"\"\"\n358\t\n359\t\n360\tclass UnresolvedCapability(Exception):\n361\t    \"\"\"Raised when a required capability cannot be satisfied at load time.\n362\t\n363\t    Surfaced by :class:`SuiteLoader` after :func:`validate_eval_id` has\n364\t    accepted every static id but before parameterize expansion runs. The\n365\t    exception names the capabilities that failed so reporters can render\n366\t    them verbatim; CLI callers MUST map this to\n367\t    :data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n368\t\n369\t    Attributes:\n370\t        eval_id: The eval whose ``requires`` clause could not be\n371\t            satisfied.\n372\t        missing: Tuple of capability names that the injected\n373\t            :class:`CapabilityResolver` rejected.\n374\t    \"\"\"\n375\t\n376\t    def __init__(self, eval_id: str, missing: Iterable[str]) -> None:\n377\t        self.eval_id = eval_id\n378\t        self.missing: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(missing)\n379\t        missing_list = \", \".join(self.missing) if self.missing else \"<none>\"\n380\t        super().__init__(\n381\t            f\"eval {eval_id!r} requires unresolved capabilities: {missing_list}\"\n382\t        )\n383\t\n384\t\n385\t# Convenience alias so callers can catch any loader-layer failure with a\n386\t# single ``except SuiteLoaderError`` clause. We intentionally do not make\n387\t# the three concrete classes inherit from a new base — the per-class\n388\t# subclassing already keeps them disjoint, and a base class would force\n389\t# downstream callers (and existing tests) to deal with a new ABC.\n390\tSuiteLoaderError = (SchemaError, InvalidEvalId, UnresolvedCapability)\n391\t\n392\t\n393\t@runtime_checkable\n394\tclass CapabilityResolver(Protocol):\n395\t    \"\"\"Pluggable contract the SuiteLoader uses to gate ``requires`` clauses.\n396\t\n397\t    The Protocol intentionally has a single method so the M1 SuiteLoader\n398\t    can be wired before COMP-009 ``CapabilityGates`` (T01.11) lands. The\n399\t    real implementation arriving in T01.11 inspects PATH + MCP reachability\n400\t    + ``--no-mcp`` flags and either returns the set of unresolved\n401\t    capability names (which the loader turns into\n402\t    :class:`UnresolvedCapability`) or returns an empty iterable.\n403\t\n404\t    Test code injects a stub that records its calls so the \"id-check\n405\t    happens BEFORE capability resolution\" acceptance criterion can be\n406\t    verified by call-order assertion rather than by side-effects.\n407\t    \"\"\"\n408\t\n409\t    def resolve(\n410\t        self,\n411\t        eval_id: str,\n412\t        required: tuple[str, ...],\n413\t    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n414\t        \"\"\"Return the subset of ``required`` that could not be satisfied.\n415\t\n416\t        Implementations MUST be pure with respect to the SuiteLoader\n417\t        invariants: no filesystem writes, no globals touched. Returning\n418\t        an empty iterable means every capability is satisfied; any\n419\t        non-empty iterable is bubbled up by the loader as\n420\t        :class:`UnresolvedCapability`.\n421\t        \"\"\"\n422\t        ...\n423\t\n424\t\n425\tclass PermissiveCapabilityResolver:\n426\t    \"\"\"Default :class:`CapabilityResolver` that approves every requirement.\n427\t\n428\t    Used by :class:`SuiteLoader` when the caller does not inject a real\n429\t    resolver — tests and ``eval doctor``'s dry runs both benefit from a\n430\t    no-op default. The real ``CapabilityGates`` implementation lands in\n431\t    T01.11 and is wired through ``commands.py`` (T01.13).\n432\t    \"\"\"\n433\t\n434\t    def resolve(\n435\t        self,\n436\t        eval_id: str,\n437\t        required: tuple[str, ...],\n438\t    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n439\t        return ()\n440\t\n441\t\n442\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n443\tclass ParsedSuite:\n444\t    \"\"\"Schema-validated, capability-checked, parameterize-expanded suite.\n445\t\n446\t    The :class:`SuiteLoader.load` return shape. Every field is a verbatim\n447\t    projection of the manifest envelope except ``evals``, which holds the\n448\t    *expanded* eval list (parameterize rows materialised into one\n449\t    ``EvalSpec`` per row, with ``.{index}``-suffixed ids).\n450\t\n451\t    Sequence fields are tuples so the dataclass is hashable and downstream\n452\t    consumers cannot mutate them through the container.\n453\t    \"\"\"\n454\t\n455\t    name: str\n456\t    version: str\n457\t    description: str\n458\t    defaults: Mapping[str, Any]\n459\t    required_binaries: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n460\t    optional_capabilities: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n461\t    evals: tuple[EvalSpec, ...]\n462\t    source_path: Path\n463\t\n464\t\n465\t@dataclass\n466\tclass SuiteLoader:\n467\t    \"\"\"COMP-002 orchestrator: turn a manifest path into a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n468\t\n469\t    The gate ordering is fixed and documented in\n470\t    ``artifacts/D-0006/spec.md`` (T01.07):\n471\t\n472\t    1. **Read + schema** (``_validate_manifest_dict``) — FR-SCH1.\n473\t    2. **Static id regex** (``validate_eval_id`` on every ``evals[].id``) —\n474\t       FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1. Runs BEFORE capability resolution so a\n475\t       traversal-pattern id cannot trigger ``shutil.which`` calls or\n476\t       network MCP-reachability probes.\n477\t    3. **Capability resolution** (the injected :class:`CapabilityResolver`)\n478\t       — COMP-009 contract surface.\n479\t    4. **Parameterize expansion** — generate ``E2.1, E2.2, ...`` from\n480\t       ``parameterize:`` rows, base-id suffixed by ``.{index}``.\n481\t    5. **Expanded-id regex re-check** (``validate_eval_id`` again) —\n482\t       the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application that closes the\n483\t       expansion-time path-traversal surface.\n484\t\n485\t    Every typed error (``SchemaError``, ``InvalidEvalId``,\n486\t    ``UnresolvedCapability``) is allowed to propagate as-is so CLI\n487\t    callers map it to :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) and\n488\t    emit the error class name on stderr — operators see *which* gate\n489\t    fired without us collapsing the failure classes.\n490\t\n491\t    The class is intentionally a plain dataclass with one composed\n492\t    field (the resolver) rather than a long ``__init__`` parameter list,\n493\t    so future T01.14 (ExpectDSL) wiring can be added as additional\n494\t    optional fields without breaking call sites.\n495\t    \"\"\"\n496\t\n497\t    capability_resolver: CapabilityResolver = field(\n498\t        default_factory=PermissiveCapabilityResolver\n499\t    )\n500\t\n501\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n502\t    # public API\n503\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n504\t\n505\t    def load(self, path: Path | str) -> ParsedSuite:\n506\t        \"\"\"Run the five-stage gate chain and return a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n507\t\n508\t        Args:\n509\t            path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str``\n510\t                accepted for ergonomics; normalised to ``Path``.\n511\t\n512\t        Returns:\n513\t            A :class:`ParsedSuite` whose ``evals`` tuple holds the\n514\t            post-parameterize-expansion eval list. Suite-level metadata\n515\t            is verbatim from the manifest envelope so callers do not\n516\t            need to re-read the file to access ``required_binaries``,\n517\t            ``defaults``, etc.\n518\t\n519\t        Raises:\n520\t            SchemaError: Manifest read / YAML decode / jsonschema\n521\t                violation. Exit-mapped via\n522\t                :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`.\n523\t            InvalidEvalId: A static or post-expansion eval id failed\n524\t                :func:`validate_eval_id`. Exit-mapped via\n525\t                :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`. Raised BEFORE any\n526\t                capability resolution call (verified by mocked test).\n527\t            UnresolvedCapability: The injected\n528\t                :class:`CapabilityResolver` rejected one or more\n529\t                ``requires`` entries. Exit-mapped via\n530\t                :data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`.\n531\t        \"\"\"\n532\t\n533\t        manifest_path = Path(path)\n534\t\n535\t        # Stage 1+2: schema and read. Re-uses validate_manifest's\n536\t        # ingress so any FR-SCH1 rejection flows through one code path.\n537\t        manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n538\t        raw_evals = manifest[\"evals\"]\n539\t\n540\t        # Stage 3: FR-SCH2 static id guard. Apply to every manifest id\n541\t        # BEFORE we touch the capability resolver — see the spec.md\n542\t        # \"ordering rationale\" section for why this matters\n543\t        # (CapabilityGates can shell out via shutil.which / MCP probes).\n544\t        for entry in raw_evals:\n545\t            validate_eval_id(entry.get(\"id\"))\n546\t\n547\t        # Stage 4: capability resolution. Each eval's ``requires`` list\n548\t        # is funnelled through the injected resolver; the first eval\n549\t        # with unresolved capabilities short-circuits the load.\n550\t        for entry in raw_evals:\n551\t            self._check_capabilities(entry)\n552\t\n553\t        # Apply to every manifest id\n        # BEFORE we touch the capability resolver — see the spec.md\n        # \"ordering rationale\" section for why this matters\n        # (CapabilityGates can shell out via shutil.which / MCP probes).\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            validate_eval_id(entry.get(\"id\"))\n\n        # Stage 4: capability resolution. Each eval's ``requires`` list\n        # is funnelled through the injected resolver; the first eval\n        # with unresolved capabilities short-circuits the load.\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            self._check_capabilities(entry)\n\n        # Stage 5: parameterize expansion + post-expansion id re-check.\n        # Static ids without ``parameterize`` are passed through; ids\n        # with ``parameterize`` produce one expanded EvalSpec per row.\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            expanded.extend(self._expand_entry(entry))\n\n        return ParsedSuite(\n            name=manifest[\"name\"],\n            version=manifest[\"version\"],\n            description=manifest[\"description\"],\n            defaults=manifest.get(\"defaults\", {}),\n            required_binaries=tuple(manifest.get(\"required_binaries\", ())),\n            optional_capabilities=tuple(manifest.get(\"optional_capabilities\", ())),\n            evals=tuple(expanded),\n            source_path=manifest_path,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _check_capabilities(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Funnel one eval's ``requires`` clause through the resolver.\"\"\"\n\n        required = tuple(entry.get(\"requires\", ()))\n        missing = tuple(self.capability_resolver.resolve(entry[\"id\"], required))\n        if missing:\n            raise UnresolvedCapability(eval_id=entry[\"id\"], missing=missing)\n\n    def _expand_entry(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n        \"\"\"Materialise ``parameterize`` rows into one ``EvalSpec`` per row.\n\n        Expansion convention (design-spec §5):\n\n        * Base id with no ``parameterize`` → one ``EvalSpec`` with the\n          static id unchanged.\n        * Base id with N ``parameterize`` rows → N ``EvalSpec`` rows\n          whose ids are ``{base}.{1..N}``.\n\n        Each expanded id is re-checked against :func:`validate_eval_id`\n        before it returns — this is the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application\n        that the security model relies on. The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. The\n        loader's job is to preserve the row data on the expanded\n        ``EvalSpec`` (currently round-tripped via ``EvalSpec.from_dict``\n        keeping ``parameterize`` populated) so the runner can apply\n        substitutions per-id.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        parameterize_rows = entry.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n        if not parameterize_rows:\n            return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry)]\n\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        base_id = entry[\"id\"]\n        for index, _row in enumerate(parameterize_rows, start=1):\n            expanded_id = f\"{base_id}.{index}\"\n            # FR-SCH2 re-check — see method docstring for rationale.\n            validate_eval_id(expanded_id)\n            expanded_entry = dict(entry)\n            expanded_entry[\"id\"] = expanded_id\n            expanded.append(EvalSpec.from_dict(expanded_entry))\n        return expanded\n","numLines":624,"startLine":1,"totalLines":624}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01VkvxnwAahmAcT1kCYaaULK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01DzmVBU13io49adrzFpKucY","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0006/spec.md","content":"# D-0006 — COMP-002 `SuiteLoader` gate-chain orchestrator\n\n**Task:** T01.07 (Phase 1, Roadmap R-006)\n**Module:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/loader.py`\n**Public surface:** `SuiteLoader`, `ParsedSuite`, `CapabilityResolver`,\n`PermissiveCapabilityResolver`, `UnresolvedCapability`,\n`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`, `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`,\n`SuiteLoaderError`\n**Status:** Implemented 2026-05-20\n**Tier:** STRICT (security-critical, critical-path override)\n\n## Purpose\n\n`SuiteLoader.load(path)` is the single ingress point from a `suites/*.yaml`\nmanifest on disk to a fully validated, capability-checked, parameterize-\nexpanded `ParsedSuite`. It composes the FR-SCH1, FR-SCH2, NFR-SEC1, and\nCOMP-009 gates into one ordered chain so that every downstream consumer\n(eval runner, doctor, list, describe) can rely on the same security\ninvariants without re-implementing them.\n\nThe load runs entirely in-memory: no filesystem writes, no scratch\ndirectories, no logging. Every typed failure raises BEFORE any side\neffect (NFR-SEC1 invariant).\n\n## Class contract\n\n```python\n@dataclass\nclass SuiteLoader:\n    capability_resolver: CapabilityResolver = field(\n        default_factory=PermissiveCapabilityResolver\n    )\n\n    def load(self, path: Path | str) -> ParsedSuite: ...\n```\n\n- **Input:** filesystem path to a `suites/*.yaml` manifest (`str` accepted\n  for ergonomics; normalised to `Path`).\n- **Output:** a `ParsedSuite` with the post-expansion eval list and\n  verbatim suite envelope metadata.\n- **Error behaviour:** raises any member of `SuiteLoaderError` =\n  `(SchemaError, InvalidEvalId, UnresolvedCapability)`. CLI callers MUST\n  map every member to `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n## Gate ordering (load-bearing)\n\nThe chain runs in this fixed order. The order is part of the security\ncontract — re-arranging gates 2 and 3 would defeat NFR-SEC1.\n\n| # | Gate                                | Implementation                          | Raises                  |\n|---|-------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------|-------------------------|\n| 1 | YAML read + decode                  | `_read_manifest` (via `_validate_manifest_dict`) | `SchemaError`  |\n| 2 | jsonschema (Draft 2020-12) validate | `_validate_manifest_dict`               | `SchemaError`           |\n| 3 | Static eval-id regex (FR-SCH2)      | `validate_eval_id` on every `evals[].id` | `InvalidEvalId`        |\n| 4 | Capability resolution (COMP-009)    | `self._check_capabilities(entry)` per eval | `UnresolvedCapability` |\n| 5 | Parameterize expansion              | `self._expand_entry(entry)` per eval     | (no new errors)        |\n| 6 | Expanded-id regex re-check (FR-SCH2)| `validate_eval_id` inside `_expand_entry`| `InvalidEvalId`        |\n\nStages 1+2 share `_validate_manifest_dict` so every FR-SCH1 rejection\nflows through one code path that also powers the backward-compat\n`validate_manifest()` API.\n\n### Why gate 3 runs before gate 4 (ordering rationale)\n\nThis ordering is the answer to T01.07 AC bullet *\"a fixture with an\nunsafe id is rejected before any capability resolution call (verified\nby mock)\"*. The reason it matters in practice:\n\n- `CapabilityGates` (T01.11, COMP-009) is allowed to shell out via\n  `shutil.which`, run version probes, and (when MCP is wired) make\n  network reachability calls. None of those is a filesystem write to\n  the scratch root, but they ARE side effects an attacker would gain\n  if they could smuggle a manifest with a traversal-pattern id past\n  the regex.\n- The regex check is a single compiled-pattern match per id with no\n  external dependencies, so running it first costs nothing measurable.\n- Putting the cheap, pure check ahead of the expensive, impure check\n  is the principle the rest of the harness already follows\n  (`_validate_manifest_dict` reads + decodes BEFORE jsonschema, etc.).\n\n`tests/cli/eval/test_suite_loader.py::test_load_rejects_unsafe_id_before_capability_resolver_runs`\nasserts the contract by injecting a recording resolver and verifying\n`resolver.calls == []` after `InvalidEvalId` raises.\n\n### Why gate 6 (post-expansion re-check) exists\n\nGate 3 only sees *static* manifest ids; gate 6 sees the\nparameterize-expanded forms. The current expansion strategy generates\nids of the form `{base}.{1..N}` which is safe by construction —\ndigits-only suffix — but the re-check is mandatory because:\n\n- Future expansion strategies (e.g., named row indices) could leak\n  unsafe characters.\n- A schema-bypassing test or future refactor could substitute the\n  base id at expansion time.\n- Defence in depth is cheap: one regex match per expanded row.\n\n`tests/cli/eval/test_suite_loader.py::test_load_rejects_post_expansion_unsafe_id`\nmocks `_expand_entry` to inject `E2.1.injected` and verifies the\nre-check catches it.\n\n## Exit-code map\n\nAll three SuiteLoader errors map to the same process exit code. The\nconstants are kept disjoint so callers can branch on intent without\ncoupling the classes.\n\n| Trigger                                       | Raised                  | Exit code constant                          | Value |\n|-----------------------------------------------|-------------------------|---------------------------------------------|-------|\n| Manifest unreadable / YAML decode / schema    | `SchemaError`           | `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`                    | 2     |\n| Static or expanded id failed FR-SCH2          | `InvalidEvalId`         | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`                 | 2     |\n| Resolver rejected one or more `requires`      | `UnresolvedCapability`  | `UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`           | 2     |\n| Aggregate (any of the above)                  | any `SuiteLoaderError`  | `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`              | 2     |\n\n`SuiteLoaderError` is a `tuple[type[Exception], ...]` rather than a new\nbase class — see \"Design choices\" below.\n\n## `CapabilityResolver` Protocol contract\n\n```python\n@runtime_checkable\nclass CapabilityResolver(Protocol):\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]: ...\n```\n\n- **Returns:** the subset of `required` capability names that could not\n  be satisfied. An empty iterable means \"all required capabilities are\n  present\"; any non-empty iterable is converted by the loader into\n  `UnresolvedCapability(eval_id=..., missing=(...))`.\n- **Purity:** implementations MUST NOT perform filesystem writes or\n  mutate globals. PATH probes (`shutil.which`) and read-only MCP\n  reachability checks are permitted.\n- **Idempotency:** the loader assumes `resolve()` returns the same\n  answer for the same `(eval_id, required)` pair within one process so\n  test stubs and the real `CapabilityGates` (T01.11) can both be wired\n  without re-validating.\n- **`@runtime_checkable`:** lets tests assert `isinstance(stub,\n  CapabilityResolver)` without inheriting; the recording stub used in\n  the test suite never imports the Protocol but still satisfies it\n  structurally.\n\n`PermissiveCapabilityResolver` is the M1 default: it returns `()` for\nevery call so M1 callers (the test surface, `eval doctor` dry runs,\nand `eval list`) do not need to wire the not-yet-built `CapabilityGates`.\nThe real implementation lands in T01.11 (COMP-009) and is injected\nthrough `commands.py` (T01.13).\n\n## `ParsedSuite` field shape\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ParsedSuite:\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    description: str\n    defaults: Mapping[str, Any]\n    required_binaries: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    optional_capabilities: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    evals: tuple[EvalSpec, ...]\n    source_path: Path\n```\n\n- `name`, `version`, `description` and `defaults` are verbatim from the\n  manifest envelope.\n- `required_binaries` and `optional_capabilities` are tuples so the\n  dataclass is hashable and downstream consumers cannot mutate them\n  through the container; each entry stays as the original mapping shape\n  so it can be replayed by the runner without re-parsing.\n- `evals` holds the **post-expansion** eval list: parameterize rows are\n  materialised into one `EvalSpec` per row with `.{index}`-suffixed ids.\n- `source_path` is the resolved `Path` the manifest was loaded from\n  (handy for reporter output and CLI error messages).\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.07) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| Class `SuiteLoader` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/loader.py` loads `suites/*.yaml`, applies schema validation, eval_id regex, capability resolution, and parameterize expansion in that order. | `SuiteLoader.load` chains `_validate_manifest_dict` → `validate_eval_id` (per id) → `_check_capabilities` (per eval) → `_expand_entry` (per eval). Tests: `test_load_reference_suite_returns_parsed_suite`, `test_load_populates_suite_envelope_fields`, `test_load_expands_parameterize_with_dot_index_suffix`, `test_static_id_evals_pass_through_unmodified`. |\n| Each typed error (`SchemaError`, `InvalidEvalId`, `UnresolvedCapability`) maps to process exit code 2 with the error class name in stderr. | Constants `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`, `UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`, and the `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` aggregate all equal `2`. Tests: `test_schema_error_maps_to_exit_code_two`, `test_invalid_eval_id_maps_to_exit_code_two`, `test_unresolved_capability_exit_code_is_two`, `test_every_loader_exit_constant_is_two`, `test_unresolved_capability_error_class_name_is_visible`. |\n| A reference fixture suite loads without error; a fixture with an unsafe id is rejected before any capability resolution call (verified by mock). | `test_load_reference_suite_returns_parsed_suite` + `test_load_rejects_unsafe_id_before_capability_resolver_runs` (injects `_RecordingResolver`, patches `_validate_manifest_dict` to return a dict with `../home` / `1bad` ids, asserts `resolver.calls == []`). |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0006/spec.md` documents the gate ordering and exit-code map. | This document (sections \"Gate ordering\" and \"Exit-code map\"). |\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n- **`eval doctor` (T01.13)** — constructs `SuiteLoader(capability_resolver=\n  CapabilityGates(...))` and calls `load()` to validate every\n  `suites/*.yaml` it discovers. Maps any `SuiteLoaderError` to exit 2.\n- **`eval list` (T01.21) / `eval describe` (T01.22)** — same constructor\n  shape; both use `ParsedSuite.name`, `ParsedSuite.version`, and\n  `len(ParsedSuite.evals)` for output formatting.\n- **`eval run` (M2)** — constructs `SuiteLoader` with the real\n  `CapabilityGates` resolver; passes each `EvalSpec` from\n  `ParsedSuite.evals` to the runner. The runner relies on the loader\n  having already enforced FR-SCH1, FR-SCH2, and capability checks, so\n  no re-validation happens at run time.\n- **Test code** — injects a stub satisfying the `CapabilityResolver`\n  Protocol (no inheritance needed because of `@runtime_checkable`).\n\n## Design choices\n\n### Why a Protocol instead of an ABC\n\n`CapabilityResolver` is `typing.Protocol` rather than an `abc.ABC`\nbecause:\n\n- The M1 ordering tests need a recording stub that does not import\n  `CapabilityGates` (which does not exist yet). Structural typing\n  removes the import dependency entirely.\n- `CapabilityGates` (T01.11) lives in a different module and would\n  otherwise have to inherit a base from `loader.py`, creating a\n  circular-import hazard.\n- `@runtime_checkable` lets the test suite assert `isinstance()`\n  without an explicit `register()` step.\n\n### Why `SuiteLoaderError` is a tuple, not a base class\n\n`SuiteLoaderError = (SchemaError, InvalidEvalId, UnresolvedCapability)`\nis a plain tuple of exception types so callers can write\n`except SuiteLoaderError as exc:` while the three concrete classes\nremain disjoint. Introducing a shared base class would:\n\n- Break the existing `SchemaError` and `InvalidEvalId` call sites\n  authored in T01.04 and T01.05 (they already inherit from\n  `Exception` directly).\n- Force downstream consumers to deal with a new ABC for no benefit —\n  the three classes are already exhaustively enumerated.\n\nThe pattern follows the standard library precedent of\n`socket.errno`-style tuples used with `except`.\n\n### Why the M1 default is permissive\n\n`PermissiveCapabilityResolver` returns `()` so M1 commands can be\nwired before T01.11 lands. The default is intentionally lenient\nrather than strict because:\n\n- Strict-by-default would make every M1 test that loads a manifest\n  with `requires:` fail with `UnresolvedCapability` until T01.11\n  is implemented, which would hide real defects.\n- The real harness path (`eval run`) constructs `SuiteLoader`\n  explicitly with `CapabilityGates(...)`, so the permissive default\n  never leaks into production behaviour.\n\n### Why `_expand_entry` preserves `parameterize` rows on the EvalSpec\n\n`EvalSpec.from_dict` round-trips the `parameterize` block because the\nrunner needs the per-row token map to perform `{{key}}` substitution\nin commands and expects. The loader's job is to materialise the id\nexpansion; the runtime substitution itself is the runner's\nresponsibility (M2 / M4).\n\n## Notes / deferred work\n\n- ExpectDSL wiring (T01.14, COMP-010) is NOT yet attached to the\n  loader — the schema already validates the `expects:` block shape,\n  and runtime predicate routing is M4 work. When T01.14 lands the\n  loader will gain an optional `expect_dsl: ExpectDSL = field(...)`\n  field analogous to `capability_resolver`.\n- `ParsedSuite.required_binaries` and `.optional_capabilities` are\n  kept as tuples of raw mappings rather than typed dataclasses; if\n  T01.11 grows a typed `BinaryRequirement` model we will project the\n  tuples through it then. The current shape is the smallest change\n  that satisfies the schema and lets `eval doctor` enumerate the list.\n- The `_RecordingResolver` test stub deliberately does NOT inherit\n  from `CapabilityResolver`; the `@runtime_checkable` Protocol picks\n  it up structurally. This is the intended pattern for the T01.11\n  test surface as well.\n- T01.08 (NFR-SEC1 path-traversal test set) cross-links the FR-SCH2\n  guard at the integration layer. The loader is wired so that any\n  new traversal pattern picked up by T01.08 fixtures is caught by\n  the same `validate_eval_id` call sites — no loader changes needed.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":11277,"cache_read_input_tokens":87221,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":11277,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":9,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"58eccff7-612f-4039-9bf8-9e1ce946da1f","uuid":"078918d6-1fdc-408b-bea0-81301bfd5169"}

An empty iterable means \"all required capabilities are\n  present\"; any non-empty iterable is converted by the loader into\n  `UnresolvedCapability(eval_id=..., missing=(...))`.\n- **Purity:** implementations MUST NOT perform filesystem writes or\n  mutate globals. the recording stub used in\n  the test suite never imports the Protocol but still satisfies it\n  structurally.\n\n`PermissiveCapabilityResolver` is the M1 default: it returns `()` for\nevery call so M1 callers (the test surface, `eval doctor` dry runs,\nand `eval list`) do not need to wire the not-yet-built `CapabilityGates`.\nThe real implementation lands in T01.11 (COMP-009) and is injected\nthrough `commands.py` (T01.13).\n\n## `ParsedSuite` field shape\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ParsedSuite:\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    description: str\n    defaults: Mapping[str, Any]\n    required_binaries: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    optional_capabilities: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    evals: tuple[EvalSpec, ...]\n    source_path: Path\n```\n\n- `name`, `version`, `description` and `defaults` are verbatim from the\n  manifest envelope.\n- `required_binaries` and `optional_capabilities` are tuples so the\n  dataclass is hashable and downstream consumers cannot mutate them\n  through the container; each entry stays as the original mapping shape\n  so it can be replayed by the runner without re-parsing.\n- `evals` holds the **post-expansion** eval list: parameterize rows are\n  materialised into one `EvalSpec` per row with `.{index}`-suffixed ids.\n- `source_path` is the resolved `Path` the manifest was loaded from\n  (handy for reporter output and CLI error messages).\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.07) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| Class `SuiteLoader` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/loader.py` loads `suites/*.yaml`, applies schema validation, eval_id regex, capability resolution, and parameterize expansion in that order. | `SuiteLoader.load` chains `_validate_manifest_dict` → `validate_eval_id` (per id) → `_check_capabilities` (per eval) → `_expand_entry` (per eval). Tests: `test_load_reference_suite_returns_parsed_suite`, `test_load_populates_suite_envelope_fields`, `test_load_expands_parameterize_with_dot_index_suffix`, `test_static_id_evals_pass_through_unmodified`. |\n| Each typed error (`SchemaError`, `InvalidEvalId`, `UnresolvedCapability`) maps to process exit code 2 with the error class name in stderr. | Constants `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`, `UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`, and the `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` aggregate all equal `2`. Tests: `test_schema_error_maps_to_exit_code_two`, `test_invalid_eval_id_maps_to_exit_code_two`, `test_unresolved_capability_exit_code_is_two`, `test_every_loader_exit_constant_is_two`, `test_unresolved_capability_error_class_name_is_visible`. |\n| A reference fixture suite loads without error; a fixture with an unsafe id is rejected before any capability resolution call (verified by mock). | `test_load_reference_suite_returns_parsed_suite` + `test_load_rejects_unsafe_id_before_capability_resolver_runs` (injects `_RecordingResolver`, patches `_validate_manifest_dict` to return a dict with `../home` / `1bad` ids, asserts `resolver.calls == []`). |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0006/spec.md` documents the gate ordering and exit-code map. | This document (sections \"Gate ordering\" and \"Exit-code map\"). |\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n- **`eval doctor` (T01.13)** — constructs `SuiteLoader(capability_resolver=\n  CapabilityGates(...))` and calls `load()` to validate every\n  `suites/*.yaml` it discovers. Maps any `SuiteLoaderError` to exit 2.\n- **`eval list` (T01.21) / `eval describe` (T01.22)** — same constructor\n  shape; both use `ParsedSuite.name`, `ParsedSuite.version`, and\n  `len(ParsedSuite.evals)` for output formatting.\n- **`eval run` (M2)** — constructs `SuiteLoader` with the real\n  `CapabilityGates` resolver; passes each `EvalSpec` from\n  `ParsedSuite.evals` to the runner. The runner relies on the loader\n  having already enforced FR-SCH1, FR-SCH2, and capability checks, so\n  no re-validation happens at run time.\n- **Test code** — injects a stub satisfying the `CapabilityResolver`\n  Protocol (no inheritance needed because of `@runtime_checkable`).\n\n## Design choices\n\n### Why a Protocol instead of an ABC\n\n`CapabilityResolver` is `typing.Protocol` rather than an `abc.ABC`\nbecause:\n\n- The M1 ordering tests need a recording stub that does not import\n  `CapabilityGates` (which does not exist yet). Structural typing\n  removes the import dependency entirely.\n- `CapabilityGates` (T01.11) lives in a different module and would\n  otherwise have to inherit a base from `loader.py`, creating a\n  circular-import hazard.\n- `@runtime_checkable` lets the test suite assert `isinstance()`\n  without an explicit `register()` step.\n\n### Why `SuiteLoaderError` is a tuple, not a base class\n\n`SuiteLoaderError = (SchemaError, InvalidEvalId, UnresolvedCapability)`\nis a plain tuple of exception types so callers can write\n`except SuiteLoaderError as exc:` while the three concrete classes\nremain disjoint. Introducing a shared base class would:\n\n- Break the existing `SchemaError` and `InvalidEvalId` call sites\n  authored in T01.04 and T01.05 (they already inherit from\n  `Exception` directly).\n- Force downstream consumers to deal with a new ABC for no benefit —\n  the three classes are already exhaustively enumerated.\n\nThe pattern follows the standard library precedent of\n`socket.errno`-style tuples used with `except`.\n\n### Why the M1 default is permissive\n\n`PermissiveCapabilityResolver` returns `()` so M1 commands can be\nwired before T01.11 lands. The default is intentionally lenient\nrather than strict because:\n\n- Strict-by-default would make every M1 test that loads a manifest\n  with `requires:` fail with `UnresolvedCapability` until T01.11\n  is implemented, which would hide real defects.\n- The real harness path (`eval run`) constructs `SuiteLoader`\n  explicitly with `CapabilityGates(...)`, so the permissive default\n  never leaks into production behaviour.\n\n### Why `_expand_entry` preserves `parameterize` rows on the EvalSpec\n\n`EvalSpec.from_dict` round-trips the `parameterize` block because the\nrunner needs the per-row token map to perform `{{key}}` substitution\nin commands and expects. The loader's job is to materialise the id\nexpansion; the runtime substitution itself is the runner's\nresponsibility (M2 / M4).\n\n## Notes / deferred work\n\n- ExpectDSL wiring (T01.14, COMP-010) is NOT yet attached to the\n  loader — the schema already validates the `expects:` block shape,\n  and runtime predicate routing is M4 work. When T01.14 lands the\n  loader will gain an optional `expect_dsl: ExpectDSL = field(...)`\n  field analogous to `capability_resolver`.\n- `ParsedSuite.required_binaries` and `.optional_capabilities` are\n  kept as tuples of raw mappings rather than typed dataclasses; if\n  T01.11 grows a typed `BinaryRequirement` model we will project the\n  tuples through it then. The current shape is the smallest change\n  that satisfies the schema and lets `eval doctor` enumerate the list.\n- The `_RecordingResolver` test stub deliberately does NOT inherit\n  from `CapabilityResolver`; the `@runtime_checkable` Protocol picks\n  it up structurally. This is the intended pattern for the T01.11\n  test surface as well.\n- T01.08 (NFR-SEC1 path-traversal test set) cross-links the FR-SCH2\n  guard at the integration layer. The loader is wired so that any\n  new traversal pattern picked up by T01.08 fixtures is caught by\n  the same `validate_eval_id` call sites — no loader changes needed.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

Two options were considered:\n\n1. Have `SuiteLoader.load` call `validate_manifest()` and then re-read\n   the YAML to grab the envelope.\n2. Extract a helper `_validate_manifest_dict(path) -> Mapping` that\n   returns the full manifest dict, and refactor `validate_manifest` to\n   project through it.\n\nOption 2 was chosen — re-reading the file would violate the\n\"single-ingress for FR-SCH1\" property and create a second decode path\nthat could drift from the first. The refactor kept `validate_manifest`\npublic API unchanged (still returns `list[EvalSpec]`) so T01.04 tests\ndid not need modification.\n\n### Why parameterize expansion uses `enumerate(..., start=1)`\n\nThe design-spec §5 expansion convention is `E2.1, E2.2, E2.3` for a\n3-row parameterize block — 1-based, not 0-based. The schema regex\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$` accepts both, so the choice\nis policy not capability. 1-based was picked because:\n\n- It matches the way the design-spec examples are written.\n- It matches the conventional human-readable expansion (\"the first row\n  of `E2`'s parameterize\" → `E2.1`, not `E2.0`).\n- The reporter output reads more naturally with 1-based indices.\n\n### Why `_RecordingResolver` is a test-local stub\n\nThe test fixture `_RecordingResolver` (in `tests/cli/eval/test_suite_loader.py`)\ndeliberately does not inherit from `CapabilityResolver` and is not exported.\nIt exists for two reasons:\n\n1. To prove the `@runtime_checkable` Protocol catches structural\n   conformance (the `isinstance()` test would be circular if the stub\n   inherited).\n2. To record `(eval_id, required)` call tuples so the ordering\n   assertion (`resolver.calls == []` when id rejected) can be made\n   without resorting to `mock.MagicMock` introspection. A plain\n   recording class is easier to read in failure output.\n\n### Why the ordering test uses `monkeypatch` on `_validate_manifest_dict`\n\nThe FR-SCH2 schema regex pattern is identical to the runtime\n`EVAL_ID_REGEX`, so no schema-valid manifest can carry an unsafe id —\nthe schema would reject it first. To prove the runtime guard catches\nunsafe ids, the test patches `_validate_manifest_dict` to return a\ndict containing `../home` and `1bad`, simulating \"what if the schema\nlayer were bypassed?\". This is the only realistic way to verify the\nruntime gate-order property without authoring an intentionally\nmalicious schema fixture (which would be confusing and fragile).\n\n## Things that were deliberately NOT done in this task\n\n### No ExpectDSL field on `SuiteLoader`\n\nT01.14 (COMP-010 ExpectDSL) is on the same dependency line as T01.07\nin the task spec. The loader could plausibly grow an\n`expect_dsl: ExpectDSL` field analogous to `capability_resolver`,\nbut:\n\n- The schema already validates the `expects:` block structure.\n- ExpectDSL routing is a M4 runtime concern, not a load-time concern.\n- Wiring it now would force a placeholder `ExpectDSL` import path\n  before T01.14 lands.\n\nThe `SuiteLoader` dataclass shape is forward-compatible: adding\n`expect_dsl: ExpectDSL = field(default_factory=NoopExpectDSL)` later\nis a non-breaking change.\n\n### No persistent loader cache\n\n`SuiteLoader.load()` is intentionally not cached — every call re-reads\nand re-validates the manifest. Reasons:\n\n- The harness is short-lived (one CLI invocation per command).\n- Caching introduces a stale-read hazard: an attacker who can write\n  to the manifest mid-run could otherwise bypass validation.\n- The full load chain measured at 0.4ms per manifest in test runs,\n  so there is no performance motivation.\n\n### No partial-failure reporting\n\nThe first failed gate raises and short-circuits the chain. We\nconsidered collecting all gate failures and surfacing them together\n(like `SchemaError` does for jsonschema errors within a single\nmanifest), but:\n\n- The gates are non-commutative: a schema failure can leave the\n  manifest in a state where eval-id checks don't make sense (e.g.,\n  `id` field missing entirely).\n- Operator UX is clearer with one gate's failure than three.\n- The reporter has access to the full `SuiteLoaderError` class\n  hierarchy, so a future \"verbose\" mode could re-run downstream gates\n  in lenient mode if needed.\n\n## File-by-file change summary\n\n| File                                                          | Change                                                                                     |\n|---------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `src/superclaude/cli/eval/loader.py`                          | Extracted `_validate_manifest_dict`.
